# Codex conversation

## User

# Codex Task: Build an OpenRoads Designer USGS Terrain Downloader

## Objective

Build a new OpenRoads Designer 2026 VBA application called **USGS Terrain Downloader** in my existing repository:

https://github.com/colewinstead/OpenRoads_vba

The purpose is to let a civil engineer draw or select a geographic boundary directly inside OpenRoads Designer, automatically identify USGS elevation data covering that boundary, and download and process that data for use in the current ORD project.

I want this to feel like a native CAD tool rather than a collection of scripts.

## 1. Inspect the existing repository first

Before implementing anything, thoroughly review:

- `PerpendicularLineGenerator/`
- `PerpendicularLineGeneratorMain.bas`
- `clsPerpendicularLocate.cls`
- `frmPerpendicularLines.code.vba`
- `modPerpendicularGeometry.bas`
- `API_NOTES.md`
- The repository README and existing VBA installation instructions.

Study how the Perpendicular Line Generator implements graphical selection, dynamic previews, custom VBA forms, Bentley API calls, input validation, and error handling.

Reuse established patterns wherever appropriate, but do not unnecessarily couple the two applications.

Place the new application in:

`USGSTerrainDownloader/`

Do not modify or break any existing VBA tools.

## 2. OpenRoads graphical boundary selection

Implement a VBA command that allows the user to choose the area for downloading terrain data.

Support these selection methods:

1. Draw a rectangular boundary interactively by clicking two opposite corners in the active DGN view.
2. Select an existing closed shape.
3. Use the active fence, if supported reliably by the documented API.

The rectangle tool should show a dynamic preview while the user moves the cursor, similar to the existing Perpendicular Line Generator.

Use Bentley's documented MicroStation VBA APIs.

Do not invent API method names or rely on unverified Civil SDK calls.

Read actual DGN/model coordinates, accounting for working units and coordinate transformations.

The selection must not permanently add a rectangle to the DGN unless the user explicitly requests it.

## 3. Coordinate system detection and conversion

This is a critical engineering requirement.

The tool must attempt to determine the active DGN's geographic coordinate system and its associated horizontal units.

It must support common engineering systems, including:

- Mississippi State Plane East and West.
- NAD83 and NAD83(2011).
- US survey feet.
- International feet.
- Meters.
- Other properly defined EPSG coordinate systems.

Convert the selected area into WGS84 geographic coordinates for the USGS search.

Use a reliable CRS transformation library such as PROJ/pyproj where appropriate.

Transform and densify boundary edges before calculating the geographic bounding box; do not assume that converting two corners is always sufficient.

If the DGN's GCS is missing or ambiguous, require the user to explicitly provide and confirm the source CRS. Never silently guess from coordinate values or geographic location.

Validate that the resulting area is geographically reasonable before querying USGS.

## 4. USGS integration

Use the official USGS National Map / TNM Access API.

Documentation:

https://tnmaccess.nationalmap.gov/api/v1/docs

https://www.usgs.gov/faqs/there-api-accessing-national-map-data

Support searching for:

- USGS 3DEP 1-meter DEM.
- USGS 3DEP 1/3-arc-second DEM.
- USGS LiDAR point clouds (LAS/LAZ).

Query available datasets using the geographic bounding area.

Do not hardcode sample download URLs or assume a particular dataset is available.

Use the API's current documented dataset identifiers, pagination, filters, and product metadata.

Handle missing coverage, API outages, failed requests, timeouts, duplicate products, and unusually large result sets.

Display the available datasets before downloading.

For each dataset, show as much available metadata as possible, including resolution, acquisition or publication date, product name, horizontal/vertical reference systems, coverage, and estimated download size.

Do not assume that the newest dataset is automatically the most accurate.

## 5. Custom VBA dialog

Create a professionally organized VBA UserForm similar to the existing generator.

Suggested title:

**USGS Terrain Downloader**

Include:

**Boundary Selection**
- Draw Rectangle
- Select Existing Shape
- Use Active Fence, if verified
- Display detected geographic extent

**Data Source**
- 1-meter DEM
- 1/3-arc-second DEM
- LiDAR point cloud

**Processing**
- Clip to selected boundary
- Mosaic intersecting raster tiles
- Reproject to DGN horizontal coordinate system
- Elevation output units: original meters, feet, or US survey feet
- Preserve original downloaded files
- Prepare processed output for ORD terrain import

**Output**
- Browse for destination folder
- Default to an appropriate writable project subfolder
- Display number of intersecting files
- Display estimated download size
- Show progress and errors
- Search USGS
- Download / Process
- Cancel

Create the interface programmatically where practical, following the pattern used by `frmPerpendicularLines.code.vba`.

Keep the UI responsive and avoid unnecessarily blocking ORD.

The GUI should clearly distinguish Search, Download, and Processing states.

## 6. Processing engine

Use VBA for the OpenRoads interface and Bentley integration.

Use Python with GDAL, PROJ, and optionally PDAL for computationally intensive geospatial operations.

Do not attempt to process millions of LiDAR points directly in VBA.

Provide a reliable interface between VBA and the Python processing component, such as a validated configuration JSON file and a subprocess with progress/status files.

Do not require administrator privileges.

Allow the Python executable and GDAL environment paths to be configured.

Handle missing dependencies gracefully and document installation, especially for a Windows workstation with ORD 2026 and QGIS/OSGeo4W.

### DEM workflow

For DEM datasets:

1. Search for overlapping USGS products.
2. Present product choices and total download size.
3. Download the selected source files.
4. Verify downloads and inspect their actual spatial metadata.
5. Mosaic compatible tiles where necessary.
6. Clip the mosaic to the original selected project boundary.
7. Reproject horizontal coordinates into the DGN's coordinate system.
8. Explicitly handle elevation units independently of horizontal coordinate conversion.
9. Save the resulting GeoTIFF.
10. Write a detailed processing and source metadata report.

Avoid mixing incompatible vertical datums without an explicit, validated transformation.

Do not assume that `gdalwarp` changing a projected CRS automatically converts all raster elevation values into feet.

Retain original elevation data separately for auditability.

### LiDAR workflow

For LiDAR:

1. Find and list intersecting LAS/LAZ products.
2. Display file counts and estimated download sizes.
3. Download selected files.
4. Preserve original point clouds.
5. Read coordinate systems, point classifications, and vertical metadata.
6. Optionally use PDAL to extract ground-classified points and produce a bare-earth raster.

Never treat all LiDAR returns as bare-earth ground points.

Do not automatically classify unknown point clouds as ground.

If full point-cloud processing significantly expands the project scope, finish the DEM workflow first, then implement LiDAR as a clearly separated second phase.

## 7. OpenRoads Designer integration

After processing finishes, allow the user to:

- Open the output folder.
- Review the generated files and metadata.
- Optionally initiate raster attachment.
- Optionally prepare for ORD terrain model creation.

Investigate whether automatic raster attachment and native terrain-model creation can be implemented reliably with the documented APIs in ORD 2026.

If a feature requires the Bentley Civil SDK rather than MicroStation VBA, clearly document that limitation.

Do not invent key-ins or call undocumented methods.

Do not claim native terrain creation works until it has been successfully tested inside ORD.

The first working version must prioritize producing a validated GeoTIFF that can be manually imported into ORD.

## 8. Engineering accuracy and safety

Treat this as professional civil-engineering software.

Include safeguards for:

- Missing/incorrect DGN coordinate systems.
- Horizontal feet versus meters.
- US survey feet versus international feet.
- Vertical elevation units.
- Vertical datum differences, including NAVD88.
- Missing CRS information in downloaded products.
- NoData handling.
- Raster coverage gaps.
- Overlapping DEM products with conflicting resolutions or dates.
- Excessively large downloads.
- Network interruptions.
- Incomplete or corrupted output.
- Coordinate transformation failures.

The application should never silently change elevation values, assign an unverified datum, or claim survey-grade accuracy.

Keep source metadata and document all transformations.

Make no changes to the user's active DGN geometry, levels, workspace configuration, VBA autoload settings, or ProjectWise configuration without explicit authorization.

## 9. Testing requirements

I am not necessarily developing on a computer with ORD installed.

Therefore, separate testing into two categories.

**Tests that can run without ORD:**
- USGS API query construction and response parsing.
- Geographic bounding box calculations.
- CRS transformations.
- Unit conversions.
- Product filtering and deduplication.
- Download logic using mocked HTTP responses.
- GDAL processing using small sample raster fixtures.
- Output metadata verification.
- Error handling and failure recovery.

**Tests requiring ORD:**
- VBA compilation.
- Bentley API compatibility.
- Graphical rectangle picking.
- Existing shape selection.
- Fence selection.
- DGN GCS extraction.
- UserForm behavior.
- Interaction with the active DGN.
- Raster attachment and terrain import.

Do not mark ORD-dependent tests as passed unless they actually run in ORD.

Use a disposable test DGN for any write testing.

Provide a repeatable test plan similar to `PerpendicularLineGenerator/TEST_PLAN.md`.

## 10. Required repository deliverables

Create a self-contained directory structured approximately like this:

```text
USGSTerrainDownloader/
    README.md
    API_NOTES.md

    requirements.txt

```

Adjust this organization if the implementation warrants it.

Document exact VBA import and UserForm assembly instructions, including the final load/run key-in.

Do not fabricate a compiled `.mvba` file. Only produce or update it through an actual Bentley VBA environment.

Document the process for compiling and testing the source inside ORD 2026.

## 11. Development approach

Implement this incrementally:

**Phase 1:** Repository review, architecture, and API validation.

**Phase 2:** Graphical boundary selection and CRS extraction.

**Phase 3:** USGS product search, metadata display, and downloads.

**Phase 4:** DEM mosaicking, clipping, reprojection, elevation-unit handling, and GeoTIFF output.

**Phase 5:** LiDAR download and optional ground-point raster processing.

**Phase 6:** Optional automatic ORD raster attachment or Civil SDK integration.

Prioritize a complete, testable DEM workflow before advanced features.

Run all tests available in the current development environment.

Where Bentley installation or access is unavailable, create the implementation and integration tests without claiming to have executed them inside ORD.

## 12. Important instructions

- Read the existing repository before writing code.
- Verify Bentley API signatures rather than guessing.
- Use current official USGS documentation.
- Prefer reliable existing GIS libraries over homemade geospatial transformations.
- Preserve my existing VBA tools.
- Do not modify production DGNs.
- Do not download large datasets during automated tests.
- Do not weaken accuracy checks merely to make tests pass.
- Explain any limitations clearly.
- Make a best effort to implement the working code, not merely provide a design document.

At completion, summarize what was implemented, what was tested, what remains unverified, and exactly how I should compile and run it in OpenRoads Designer 2026.

The final goal is simple:

**Open ORD → Draw a box → Search USGS → Select elevation data → Download → Automatically create a correctly georeferenced, engineering-ready terrain raster.**

## Assistant

I’ll review the existing VBA tool and verify the Bentley and USGS interfaces first, then build the downloader in its own directory. I’ll test the Python workflow here and keep ORD-only checks explicitly marked as unverified.

    # Ponytail

    You are a lazy senior developer. The best code is the code never written. You solve the whole problem with the least new code. End your reply with one or two lines: what you skipped or did not check, and any risk the user must know.

    Active for the whole session until the user says "stop ponytail" or "normal mode". Switch level: `/ponytail lite|full|ultra`.

    ## Before you write

    Read the task and the code it touches. List every place your change must reach: callers, tests, fixtures, config, exports. Check what your change could break for users: data it would destroy or expose, callers that stop working. That is scope. Extra features are not.

    ## The smallest complete change

    Take the first option that fully works:

    1. Does it need to exist? Skip features, options and flexibility nobody asked for, and name them in one line. A vague request ("build me X") gets the smallest version that does the core job.
    2. Already in this codebase (a helper, component, service, pattern)? Use it the way the surrounding code does.
    3. Standard library or a platform feature? Use it, unless the project has its own. A house component beats a native widget.
    4. An installed dependency? Use it. Never add a dependency for a few lines.
    5. Can it be one line a reader gets at a glance? One line.
    6. Otherwise: the minimum code that works.

    - Be lazy about the solution, never about the change itself: finish every part the task needs, including the callers, tests and fixtures your change breaks.
    - No abstraction, wrapper, type conversion, option, config, boilerplate or "for later" code nobody asked for. Keep values in the form the platform already gives you. Deletion beats addition. Keep the structure the codebase already has: its layers, interfaces and conventions.
    - The shortest working diff wins, once you know everything it must touch. A one-liner that needs decoding is not short.
    - Comment only the why the code cannot show, in one line.
    - Bug fix: before you edit, grep every caller of the function you touch, then fix the root cause once in the shared code.
    - Code you move or merge keeps its error handling and validation.
    - Between options of equal size, take the one that is correct on edge cases.
    - Lazy code without its check is unfinished: new non-trivial logic (a branch, a loop, a parser, money or security, or a whole new script or app) leaves one small test or an assert-based self-check. Trivial changes need none.
    - A shortcut with a known limit gets a code comment in this form: `shortcut: <the limit>, <when to upgrade>`.

    Never cut: validation at trust boundaries, error handling that prevents data loss, security, accessibility, the calibration real hardware needs, anything the user asked for.

    ## Levels

    | Level | Behavior |
    |-------|----------|
    | **lite** | Build what was asked. Name the smaller option in one line and let the user pick. |
    | **full** | The rules above. Default. |
    | **ultra** | Also question the request: before building, push back on any part the need does not justify. |

    # Civil engineering

    Use the relevant engineering knowledge below while preserving the requested scope. This skill is not a governing standard or a substitute for engineering judgment or the Engineer of Record.

    ## Core constraints

    - Never invent criteria, equations, tolerances, design values, or agency requirements. Distinguish authoritative requirements, recommended practices, mathematical results, conventions, project criteria, assumptions, and implementation decisions. Verify the applicable jurisdiction, edition, and scope before applying criteria.
    - Track units explicitly, including US survey feet versus international feet, meters/inches, percent versus decimal slope, and degrees/radians. Never silently assume CRS, EPSG, datum/realization, geoid, projection, axis order, horizontal units, or elevation units.
    - Preserve engineering precision internally; round for a stated purpose and avoid false precision in outputs. Select tolerances from an error budget and never weaken them merely to pass a test.
    - Prefer analytical geometry where available. Disclose and bound necessary numerical integration or sampling. Unsupported, malformed, contradictory, or ambiguous inputs need useful diagnostics; never silently substitute plausible geometry or criteria.
    - Treat independently validated behavior as a regression constraint unless its intended behavior is explicitly changed. Software tests alone do not prove engineering correctness.
    - Separate calculation state from presentation. UI-only changes must preserve engineering results; reports, drawings, and exports must describe the same calculation and assumptions.

    ## Select references

    Read only the row matching the task. Add another reference only for an actual dependency; do not load this entire directory.

    | Task | Start here | Add only when needed |
    |---|---|---|
    | Horizontal geometry, tangents/arcs/spirals | [Roadway geometry](references/roadway-geometry.md) | Stationing or LandXML |
    | Profiles, grades, vertical curves | [Vertical geometry](references/vertical-geometry.md) | Coordinate systems |
    | Station equations, nearest point, LT/RT | [Stationing and offsets](references/stationing-offsets.md) | Roadway geometry |
    | Crown, runoff/runout, design tables | [Superelevation](references/superelevation.md) | Roadway geometry |
    | Guardrail, terminals, bridge approaches | [Guardrail](references/guardrail.md) | Stationing for alignment placement |
    | Culverts, drainage, hydraulics/hydrology | [Drainage](references/drainage.md) | Applicable external method for uncovered topics |
    | LandXML import/interpretation | [LandXML](references/landxml.md) | Applicable geometry or terrain reference |
    | TIN, raster, GeoTIFF | [Terrain surfaces](references/terrain-surfaces.md) | CRS and GIS/CAD for conversion |
    | CRS, EPSG, State Plane, transformations | [Coordinate systems](references/coordinate-systems.md) | Field data for GNSS |
    | GPS, geotagged photos, field evidence | [Surveying and field data](references/surveying-field-data.md) | CRS for project coordinates |
    | CAD/GIS exports or conversion QA | [GIS/CAD interoperability](references/gis-cad-interoperability.md) | Applicable domain |
    | Base-section quantities | [Quantities](references/quantities.md) | Applicable project specification |
    | Engineering software change/review | [Engineering QA](references/engineering-software-qa.md) | Only the affected domain |
    | Evidence, authority, conflicting implementations | [Provenance](references/provenance.md) | Only the cited source needed |

    ## Working method

    Identify what engineering behavior can change, its units/spatial context, assumptions, criteria, and existing validated baseline. Inspect the current calculation path before editing. Check mathematical edge cases, geometry boundaries, ambiguity, conversions, and malformed inputs. Validate against analytical solutions, round trips within their valid domain, independent implementations, known software/data, or authoritative examples as available.

    Report numerical differences with units and classify them as expected changes, precision effects, implementation errors, changed assumptions, or changed engineering behavior. State remaining authoritative verification needs and the limits of the evidence. Reference labels distinguish general/math knowledge, safeguards, validated behavior, agency/project criteria, application/implementation behavior, assumptions, and unknown authority; definitions and source IDs are in provenance.

    # OpenRoads_vba

    Bentley OpenRoads Designer / MicroStation VBA macros. The original
    `nodeincell.mvba` and `PerpendicularLineGenerator/PatternLineGenerator.mvba`
    ship compiled. The tools also include reviewable **VBA source** where available
    (`.bas`, with class/form source where a tool needs them).

    **Want it packaged?** [**OpenRoads Field Kit**](https://fingazpdx.gumroad.com/l/openroads-field-kit) — the same macros as a one-click zip with a quick-start guide, printable cheat sheet, and email support. The code in this repo is MIT and stays free; the kit is packaging and a reply when something breaks.

    The macros target plain MicroStation VBA so they run in any OpenRoads
    Designer release without depending on a specific CivilModel API version.
    A few (StationOffset, PointsIO) are written as conservative fallbacks for
    the OpenRoads-native annotation tools.

    ## Importing

    1. Clone or download the repo.
    2. In OpenRoads Designer: open the **VBA Project Manager**.
    3. Right-click an existing project (or create a new one) → **File →
       Import File...** → pick a `.bas` file.
    4. Each module appears under your project. Run a sub with:

       ```
       VBA RUN [ProjectName] ModuleName.SubName
       ```

       For example: `VBA RUN MyTools LabelAcres.LabelAcresMain`

    5. Bind frequent commands to function keys via **Workspace → Function
       Keys** for one-keystroke access.

    ## Adding a VBA macro to a ribbon button (ORD / ProjectWise)

    You can add a custom ribbon button that **loads a VBA project when clicked and then runs its macro**. This is useful when switching between ProjectWise managed workspaces, where VBA autoload configuration may not persist or may interfere with workspace compilation.

    1. Save your compiled `.mvba` file in an accessible, local or synced folder. If using OneDrive, make the file **Always keep on this device**.
    2. In ORD's **Key-in** window, test the VBA load and run commands separately:

       ```text
       vba load "C:\CAD_Custom\VBA\RotateElementHorizontal.mvba"
       vba run [RotateElementHorizontal]Main
       ```

    3. Test the combined key-in below on a disposable element in a test DGN. Replace the example path, project name, and procedure with those for your macro:

       ```text
       vba load "C:\CAD_Custom\VBA\RotateElementHorizontal.mvba";vba run [RotateElementHorizontal]Main
       ```

       The semicolon separates the two key-ins; depending on your ORD settings, you may need **Parse All Mode** enabled. The quoted path accommodates spaces and punctuation in folder names.

    4. Right-click the ORD ribbon and choose **Customize Ribbon**. In a personal ribbon customization, add a group and button (or edit an existing button). Set the button's **Key-in** to the tested combined command, give it a label and icon, and click **Apply**.
    5. Restart ORD and test the button in another DGN or ProjectWise project. Back up your personal ribbon `.dgnlib` independently of your `.upf` preferences file.

    ### ProjectWise managed-workspace warning

    **Avoid adding personal, absolute VBA paths to `MS_VBAAUTOLOADPROJECTS` or `MS_VBASEARCHDIRECTORIES` in ProjectWise Personal WorkSpace settings without CAD administration approval.** In a managed workspace, such settings can cause **Error 222: Failed to generate managed workspace configuration file** during workspace compilation. The button-based load-and-run method above does not require these configuration entries.

    If this error occurs after adding personal VBA paths:

    - Use **ProjectWise Explorer → Tools → Options → Personal WorkSpace** to remove **only the custom entries you added**, leaving the organization's existing configuration intact.
    - Do not edit automatically generated ProjectWise workspace `.cfg` files (which can be regenerated), and do not delete `.upf` or ribbon `.dgnlib` files to troubleshoot this issue.
    - If you cannot access or safely edit the Personal WorkSpace settings, contact your ProjectWise/CAD administrator.

    > **Note:** `RotateElementHorizontal.mvba` is an example project from an external workspace; it is **not included in this repository**. Use a VBA project you have permission to run and distribute.

    ## Routines

    ### Parcel / area work

    | Sub                          | File                       | What it does                                                                 |
    |------------------------------|----------------------------|------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
    | `LabelAcresMain`             | `LabelAcres.bas`           | Label every selected closed shape with **area in acres to 0.1 AC** at the bbox center. |
    | `TotalAreaMain`              | `TotalArea.bas`            | Sum area of every selected closed shape; report sq ft + acres.                |
    | `TotalLengthMain`            | `TotalLength.bas`          | Sum length of every selected linear element; report ft + mi.                  |
    | `LotBDMain`                  | `LotBearingDistance.bas`   | Label every straight segment of the first selected shape with bearing+distance, plus area at centroid. |

    ### Survey / point I/O

    | Sub                          | File                       | What it does                                                                 |
    |------------------------------|----------------------------|------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
    | `ImportPNEZDMain`            | `PointsIO.bas`             | Read a PNEZD CSV and place zero-length lines + 3-line text nodes for each point. |
    | `ExportPNEZDMain`            | `PointsIO.bas`             | Write every selected text node out to PNEZD CSV (first line treated as PNO).  |
    | `CoordNEMain`                | `CoordLabel.bas`           | Two-line Northing / Easting label at the first selected element's centroid.    |
    | `CoordElevMain`              | `CoordLabel.bas`           | One-line `EL ###.##` label at the first selected element's centroid.           |
    | `PointGridMain`              | `PointGrid.bas`            | Rectangular grid of zero-length point elements between two corners.            |
    | `StationOffsetMain`          | `StationOffset.bas`        | Station & offset (L/R) of a target X,Y relative to a selected alignment polyline. |

    ### COGO

    | Sub                          | File                       | What it does                                                                 |
    |------------------------------|----------------------------|------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
    | `InverseReportMain`          | `InverseReport.bas`        | Full inverse on the first selected line: bearing, distance, dN/dE/dZ, slope.  |
    | `SlopeLabelMain`             | `SlopeLabel.bas`           | Slope label at the midpoint of a selected line (percent + H:V ratio).         |
    | `Main`                      | [PerpendicularLineGenerator](PerpendicularLineGenerator/README.md) | ORD 2026 cross-lines at cumulative geometry distances; settings UserForm, forward/reverse, centered/left/right, active level/symbology. |

    #### Perpendicular Line Generator (ORD 2026)

    Load the included `PerpendicularLineGenerator/PatternLineGenerator.mvba`, a real
    project saved in ORD with its UserForm. The [installation and usage guide](PerpendicularLineGenerator/README.md)
    also includes complete module/class source and instructions for rebuilding the
    UserForm from `frmPerpendicularLines.code.vba`. A `.bas` import alone does not
    create this dialog.

    ```text
    VBA LOAD "C:\CAD_Custom\VBA\PatternLineGenerator.mvba"
    VBA RUN [PatternLineGenerator]PerpendicularLineGeneratorMain.Main
    ```

    Replace the path with your saved file location. For a ribbon button that loads
    and runs the tool, use:

    ```text
    vba load "C:\CAD_Custom\VBA\PatternLineGenerator.mvba";vba run [PatternLineGenerator]PerpendicularLineGeneratorMain.Main
    ```

    Run the tool, select a horizontal alignment, then pick the start and end with
    dynamic cross-line previews before the settings dialog opens. Pick Start/Pick End
    buttons re-pick limits; Full Alignment restores the complete range. Reset during
    a pick opens settings without accepting the moving cursor location.
    Defaults are 150 master-unit spacing and 200 master-unit total length, centered,
    start 0, geometric end, starting line included, ending line excluded, forward.
    The dialog shows active level, working units, selection status and estimated
    count. In a feet-master model, a 1,000-foot straight produces seven 200-foot
    lines at distances 0 through 900 feet; each extends 100 feet on either side.

    Supported exposed VBA geometry includes lines, line strings, arcs, B-splines
    and connected complex strings. Inputs are geometry distances, **not ORD
    stationing**. Native civil spirals and station equations are not interpreted;
    displayed spline/stroke geometry does not certify native alignment accuracy.
    See the [API/support notes](PerpendicularLineGenerator/API_NOTES.md),
    [troubleshooting](PerpendicularLineGenerator/README.md#troubleshooting), and
    [repeatable ORD test plan](PerpendicularLineGenerator/TEST_PLAN.md). This tool
    uses actual model master units, including non-foot units. The user reports the
    generator and dynamic picking working in ORD; measured acceptance tests remain
    pending. It changes no ProjectWise or VBA autoload configuration.

    ### Text utilities

    | Sub                          | File                       | What it does                                                                 |
    |------------------------------|----------------------------|------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
    | `TextUpperMain`              | `TextCase.bas`             | Selected text/text-nodes → UPPER.                                             |
    | `TextLowerMain`              | `TextCase.bas`             | Selected text → lower.                                                        |
    | `TextTitleCaseMain`          | `TextCase.bas`             | Selected text → Title Case.                                                   |
    | `TextHeightMain`             | `TextHeight.bas`           | Apply a new height (master units) to every selected text/node.                |
    | `TextRotateHorizMain`        | `TextRotateHoriz.bas`      | Rotate upside-down text 180° so it reads left-to-right.                       |

    ### Z / elevation

    | Sub                          | File                       | What it does                                                                 |
    |------------------------------|----------------------------|------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
    | `FlattenZMain`               | `ZTools.bas`               | Set Z = 0 on every vertex/origin of every selected element.                   |
    | `MoveZMain`                  | `ZTools.bas`               | Add a delta to Z across the selection.                                        |
    | `SetZMain`                   | `ZTools.bas`               | Set Z to a single value across the selection.                                 |

    ### Levels & cells

    | Sub                          | File                       | What it does                                                                 |
    |------------------------------|----------------------------|------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
    | `LevelCountMain`             | `LevelCount.bas`           | Element counts grouped by level (graphical elements only).                    |
    | `LevelTranslateMain`         | `LevelTranslate.bas`       | CSV-driven level rename / re-leveler (`OLD,NEW` per row, auto-creates).       |
    | `LevelIsolateMain`           | `LevelIsolate.bas`         | Turn display off on every level except the selected element's level.          |
    | `LevelShowAllMain`           | `LevelIsolate.bas`         | Turn display on for every level.                                              |
    | `CellCountMain`              | `CellCount.bas`            | Tally of placed cells grouped by cell name.                                   |

    ### Original

    | File                | What it does                                                                       |
    |---------------------|------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
    | `nodeincell.mvba`   | Copy + increment text inside a cell as a single operation (compiled VBA project).   |

    ## Notes

    - **Working units** are assumed to be survey **feet**. For metric DGNs,
      change `SQFT_PER_ACRE` in `LabelAcres.bas`, `TotalArea.bas`, and
      `LotBearingDistance.bas` to `4046.856` (sq m / acre).
    - **`StationOffset`** is a fallback for environments where the OpenRoads
      CivilModel API isn't loaded. For real alignment objects with stationing
      equations and superelevation, prefer the native OpenRoads
      **Civil Annotation** tools — this VBA only understands plain MicroStation
      geometry.
    - **`LevelIsolate`** writes to `Level.IsDisplayed`. If your DGN is
      attached to references with their own level override masks, the
      references won't update — toggle Level Display on the reference itself.
    - **`ImportPNEZD` / `ExportPNEZD`** treat the second line of a text node
      as the elevation when round-tripping. The import-then-export pair is
      lossless within `Format("0.000")` precision.
    - Source modules use `Option Explicit` and `On Error GoTo Cleanup` so
      errors surface with a clear message instead of silently corrupting
      geometry.

    ## Sister repos

    Vanilla AutoCAD / Civil 3D LISP equivalents of many of these macros
    (LABELACRES, BD, SLP, NE, ZL, CHZ, FLAT, etc.) live in
    [C3D-AutoCAD](https://github.com/mf4633/C3D-AutoCAD). Carlson Civil/Survey
    equivalents (LABELAC, LOTBD, PNORENUM, PNOINV, TRAV, FBREP, STALBL, etc.)
    live in [Carlson-CAD](https://github.com/mf4633/Carlson-CAD). Together the
    three repos cover the LISP / VBA tooling layer of most US civil offices.

    ## Contributing

    One `.bas` per logical unit (one module, one or more closely related Subs).
    Header comment names the Sub(s), the key-in syntax, and any assumptions
    about working units. Append a row to the right table above.

    ## See also

    Companion calculators for corridor design: [AASHTO superelevation](https://pe-calc.com/tools/superelevation.html) and [vertical curve](https://pe-calc.com/tools/vertical-curve.html) on pe-calc.com.
    # Perpendicular Line Generator

    An ORD 2026 / MicroStation VBA tool that places independent straight cross-lines
    at cumulative **geometry path distances**, on the active level with active
    symbology. No source alignment, level definition, workspace variable, ProjectWise
    configuration, or VBA autoload setting is changed.

    **Support boundary:** this release evaluates standard VBA geometry. It does not
    read the native ORD civil schema or certify an underlying civil spiral from its
    display graphics. Native spirals that VBA cannot expose reliably require a Civil
    SDK add-in. Do not stroke a spiral into a line string to bypass this limitation.
    See [geometry and API notes](API_NOTES.md) before using civil alignments.

    ## Files

    | File | Purpose |
    | --- | --- |
    | `PatternLineGenerator.mvba` | User-built ORD project with the modules, class and assembled UserForm |
    | `PerpendicularLineGeneratorMain.bas` | Entry point, alignment/range session, capture of active settings, preflight and writing/rollback |
    | `modPerpendicularGeometry.bas` | Geometry validation, component traversal, distances, derivatives and endpoints |
    | `clsPerpendicularLocate.cls` | `ILocateCommandEvents` alignment selection and `IPrimitiveCommandEvents` range picking/dynamics |
    | `frmPerpendicularLines.code.vba` | Complete UserForm code, including construction of all controls and event handlers |
    | `modPerpendicularTests.bas` | Optional, in-memory ORD self-tests; no DGN elements added |
    | `TEST_PLAN.md` | Repeatable acceptance tests, including native spiral support gates |
    | `API_NOTES.md` | Verified Bentley API signatures, numerical policies and civil SDK alternative |

    The included `.mvba` was built and saved by the user in ORD; it is not a
    fabricated binary. Its four stored source components match the supplied source
    after normalizing VBA editor casing, whitespace and export attributes. The
    optional test module is not included in that project. The form code file is
    **not an exported `.frm`**. There is no project-editing installer.

    ## Run the included project

    1. Save `PatternLineGenerator.mvba` in an accessible folder, for example
       `C:\CAD_Custom\VBA`. If using OneDrive, choose **Always keep on this device**.
    2. Open a disposable design model in ORD 2026 and enter these key-ins, replacing
       the load path with your saved file location:

       ```text
       VBA LOAD "C:\CAD_Custom\VBA\PatternLineGenerator.mvba"
       VBA RUN [PatternLineGenerator]PerpendicularLineGeneratorMain.Main
       ```

    3. Select the alignment, pick start and end, review settings, then Generate Lines.

    For a ribbon button, test this combined key-in, then use it as the button's
    **Key-in** in **Customize Ribbon**:

    ```text
    vba load "C:\CAD_Custom\VBA\PatternLineGenerator.mvba";vba run [PatternLineGenerator]PerpendicularLineGeneratorMain.Main
    ```

    See the repository's [ribbon button instructions](../README.md#adding-a-vba-macro-to-a-ribbon-button-ord--projectwise).
    The internal project name of the included file is `PatternLineGenerator`.
    Rebuilding from source below is an alternative to loading that file.

    ## Install, assemble the form, and compile

    1. Open a disposable **design model** in ORD 2026. Work in a model containing the
       alignment itself; reference attachment coordinates are not supported.
    2. Open **VBA Project Manager** from ORD's Utilities/Macros tools. Create a **new**
       project and save it, for example as
       `C:\CAD_Custom\VBA\PerpendicularLineGenerator.mvba`. Do not import into an
       existing project unless you deliberately choose to do so.
    3. Open the VBA editor. In **Tools > [project] Properties**, set the project name
       to `PerpendicularLineGenerator`. Select that project in Project Explorer.
    4. Use **File > Import File** to import these three files:
       `PerpendicularLineGeneratorMain.bas`, `modPerpendicularGeometry.bas`, and
       `clsPerpendicularLocate.cls`. Optionally import `modPerpendicularTests.bas`.
       Remove the new project's unused empty standard module if desired.
       **If the `.cls` appears under Modules instead of Class Modules:** remove that
       incorrectly imported component, then choose **Insert > Class Module**. Press
       **F4** and set its **(Name)** to `clsPerpendicularLocate`. Open the supplied
       `.cls` in a text editor and copy only from `Option Explicit` through the end
       into the new class's code window. Do **not** paste the `VERSION`, `BEGIN/END`,
       or `Attribute` export-header lines. The resulting class must appear under
       **Class Modules**. No separate paste-source file is needed.
    5. Choose **Insert > UserForm**. In the Properties window, set its **(Name)** to
       exactly `frmPerpendicularLines`. Leave the designer blank: add **no controls**.
    6. Right-click this form and choose **View Code**. Paste the entire contents of
       `frmPerpendicularLines.code.vba` into the form's code window, replacing any
       blank default event stub. It begins with `Option Explicit`; paste it only once.
       `UserForm_Initialize` creates the complete dialog and connects its controls
       through `WithEvents` fields. **Importing the `.bas` modules alone will not
       create the form.**
    7. **Tools > References** must include ORD's existing **Bentley MicroStation DGN**
       object library and **Microsoft Forms 2.0 Object Library**. Inserting the form
       normally supplies Forms automatically. Do not add Office/Excel or Civil SDK
       references, and do not register arbitrary DLLs.
    8. Choose **Debug > Compile PerpendicularLineGenerator** and resolve any errors
       before running. Save the project. The authoring agent has not compiled or
       executed this source inside ORD; the supplied binary was saved by the user.
    9. To distribute a genuine exported form later, right-click the assembled form
       and choose **Export File**. Keep its `.frm` and accompanying `.frx` together.
       An exported pair can be imported directly in another ORD VBA project instead
       of repeating steps 5–6. Do not rename or separate that pair.

    No automatic access to `VBProject`/`VBComponents` is used or required. No trust
    setting needs to be weakened. The source has no platform DLL declarations.

    ### Update an already working project

    1. In the VBA editor, stop the macro with **Run > Reset** if it is active.
       Save/export a backup of your working project before replacing its source.
    2. Replace the two standard modules by importing the updated
       `PerpendicularLineGeneratorMain.bas` and `modPerpendicularGeometry.bas`
       after removing the old modules of the same names from **your tool's project**.
    3. Keep the existing **Class Module** `clsPerpendicularLocate`. Replace its code
       with the updated `.cls` contents **starting at `Option Explicit`**. Do not
       paste any `VERSION`, `BEGIN/END`, or `Attribute` export-header lines. The new
       body implements both `ILocateCommandEvents` and `IPrimitiveCommandEvents`.
    4. Keep the existing blank UserForm `frmPerpendicularLines`. Replace its entire
       code with the updated `frmPerpendicularLines.code.vba`. No designer controls
       need to be added; the three new buttons are created when the form initializes.
    5. If using the optional self-tests, replace that standard module too. Compile
       and save the project, then run its existing Main key-in. For a project named
       `PatternLineGenerator`, use:

       ```text
       VBA RUN [PatternLineGenerator]PerpendicularLineGeneratorMain.Main
       ```

    Editing these source files does not update an already saved/loaded `.mvba`.
    This repository update does not modify your existing `.mvba` automatically.

    ## Source-build ORD key-ins

    With the file saved at the example path and the project name set as above:

    ```text
    VBA LOAD "C:\CAD_Custom\VBA\PerpendicularLineGenerator.mvba"
    VBA RUN [PerpendicularLineGenerator]PerpendicularLineGeneratorMain.Main
    ```

    Optional self-test command, after importing the test module and ending the
    generator session (including any active range picker):

    ```text
    VBA RUN [PerpendicularLineGenerator]modPerpendicularTests.RunSelfTests
    ```

    Replace the load path with your actual `.mvba` path. The bracketed run name is
    the **internal VBA project name**. Load manually or bind these key-ins to a
    personal button; this tool does not change autoload/search configuration.

    ## Dialog and usage

    The modal dialog is titled **Perpendicular Line Generator**. A clear heading and
    units notice appear above a read-only alignment status, active-level display and
    working-unit labels. Aligned setting rows occupy the center, with endpoint
    checkboxes, a live line-count/error area, and **Full Alignment / Generate Lines /
    Cancel** below. **Pick Start** and **Pick End** buttons sit beside the distance
    inputs. While picking, the dialog hides so the DGN receives mouse input.
    The form uses Segoe UI, normal Windows controls, keyboard tab order, Enter for
    Generate and Escape for Cancel. The selection status includes path length and
    component count. No screenshot is supplied because the dialog has not been
    rendered in ORD during implementation.

    1. **Run the macro, then select the alignment.** The first prompt always starts
       graphical locate, even if elements were preselected. Identify the supported
       horizontal element and accept it with a data point. Reset with no candidate
       highlighted cancels. All components must pass the existing geometry checks.
    2. **Pick Start.** Move near the alignment to preview a perpendicular cross-line
       at the closest XY point on the actual supported curve. The status prompt shows
       geometry distance in master units. Click to accept the start location.
    3. **Pick End.** The accepted start cross-line stays in the dynamics preview while
       a second cross-line follows the mouse's closest point on the alignment. Click
       to accept an end strictly after the start in the chosen direction. This sets
       the range limit; the Include Ending Line checkbox still controls whether an
       output line is actually placed at that exact end. The settings dialog opens.
    4. Review the active level and model master units. All numeric distances use
       **master units**, regardless of the model's master/subunit display format.
    5. Adjust settings. **Pick Start** or **Pick End** hides the dialog to re-pick that
       limit, then returns to the dialog. **Full Alignment** sets start to zero and
       end to `End of Alignment`. The live estimate uses the generation distance-list
       builder. Invalid input disables Generate and displays the reason.
    6. Click **Generate Lines**. The current active level, color, line style and line
       weight are captured at this point. Every coordinate, tangent and in-memory
       `LineElement` is validated before the first `AddElement` call.
    7. Read the completion message and inspect the results. A generation error
       attempts to remove only this run's newly added elements. If cleanup fails,
       the message explicitly calls for Undo and inspection.

    **Reset/right-click during a range pick** returns to the settings dialog without
    accepting the moving cursor location. Any start already accepted remains set.
    Use this to choose Full Alignment, change Direction, adjust preview length or
    placement, or cancel. An end before/equal to start is not accepted: choose a later
    location or Reset, change Direction, and re-pick. Switching to another ORD command
    ends the pick session; run the tool again to select an alignment. Panning/zooming
    that merely suspends the command should allow picking to resume (verify in ORD).

    Preview elements are only drawn in dynamics; they are never added to the DGN.
    No output is created until Generate Lines. The preview uses the last-used total
    length, placement and direction, initially 200/centered/forward. Distances saved
    from picks retain full numeric precision; the three-decimal status display does
    not round the stored values. These are standard VBA dynamics/status prompts,
    not the native civil offset tool's floating parameter editor, Alt locks, or civil
    offset rules. The selected alignment remains untouched.

    Settings survive Cancel, the window close button, and successful runs through
    the default form instance for the current loaded VBA session. They reset when
    the project unloads, VBA is reset, or ORD closes. Values are not saved to the DGN,
    registry, or another project. Reopening the dialog refreshes alignment/level/units.

    | Setting | Initial value |
    | --- | --- |
    | Line Spacing | `150.00` |
    | Total Line Length | `200.00` |
    | Placement | Centered on Alignment |
    | Start Distance Along Alignment | `0.00` |
    | End Distance | `End of Alignment` (blank also means the geometric end) |
    | Include Starting Line | Yes |
    | Include Ending Line | No |
    | Direction | Forward |

    ### Distance and direction rules

    - Forward uses the selected element's stored direction. Reverse measures zero
      from the other end and reverses the tangent. Left and right follow the **chosen
      travel direction** looking forward in plan (global XY), not the current view.
    - Picked values use the Direction selected **at the time of the pick**. Changing
      Direction later reinterprets the numeric distances from the other end, as with
      typed distances. Re-pick both limits if you want to keep particular physical
      locations after changing direction.
    - Start/end inputs are increasing distances from that direction's geometry
      beginning. They are **not** displayed ORD stations, starting station offsets,
      or station-equation values. Enter `150`, not `1+50` or `150 ft`.
    - Regular locations are `start + k * spacing`. Excluding the starting line skips
      `k = 0`; it does not shift the spacing grid.
    - The ending line is independently controlled. When excluded, a regular-grid
      location coinciding with the end is also excluded. When included, the exact
      requested end is placed **once**, even if not on the grid. That final interval
      can therefore be shorter than the spacing.
    - Centered placement puts half the **total** line length on either side. Left
      and right placement put the full length on that side, anchored at the alignment.
    - Generated lines keep the horizontal alignment's Z; the tool does not sample a
      terrain or use the active elevation setting.

    ### Feet example

    In a model whose master unit is feet, select an eastbound 1,000-foot line, pick
    its beginning and end (or Reset and choose Full Alignment), and accept the other
    defaults: 150-foot spacing and 200-foot total line length. Seven
    centered cross-lines occur at geometry distances **0, 150, 300, 450, 600, 750,
    900 feet**. Each extends 100 feet left and 100 feet right. Including the ending
    line adds one at 1,000 feet with a 100-foot final interval.

    In a meter model the same numeric inputs mean **150 meters and 200 meters**;
    no feet conversion is applied. The exact model unit definition controls whether
    feet are international or US survey feet; the short label `ft` alone may not
    distinguish them. Inspect ORD's working-unit definition when that distinction
    matters.

    ## Supported geometry and limits

    Supported: open horizontal lines, line strings, circular/elliptical arcs, regular
    B-spline curves, and connected complex strings recursively containing only those
    types. An ORD civil alignment can be used **only to the extent its selected VBA
    element exposes this supported geometry**. The selected representation is the
    computational definition for this macro.

    Curve evaluation uses Bentley B-spline distance integration/inversion and its
    analytic local derivative. Arc and line conversions do not use strokes. Spacing
    continues through component joins and never restarts per component. Line string
    segments are used only for actual selected line string geometry, not as a
    replacement for an accessible curved element.

    Not supported: native civil schema/station equations, opaque native transition
    spirals/custom or application elements, cells, shared cells, shapes, closed paths,
    legacy type-11 curves, reference attachments, sheet models, or geometry with changing Z/tilted
    planes. Internal spline discontinuities are rejected. A requested placement at a
    non-tangent line-string/complex join is rejected rather than assigning an
    arbitrary perpendicular. Gaps and ambiguous component ordering are rejected.
    The macro does not repair, reverse, or drop the source alignment.

    Graphical picking evaluates every supported component with Bentley's closest-point
    API, then converts its returned parameter to cumulative curve length. Cursor Z
    is ignored; use plan position. Distinct equally close locations on different
    components are rejected (including a crossing), while a shared join with one
    chain distance is allowed. The API returns one minimum per individual B-spline;
    it cannot establish uniqueness for multiple equally close points inside that
    single component. Use unambiguous picks or typed distances for such geometry.
    Very large component counts may make mouse previews slow because every component
    is checked. The native civil-spiral support boundary still applies.

    A native spiral displayed as a spline or stroke may already be an approximation.
    VBA geometry alone cannot prove equivalence to its civil definition. Do not use
    the output for native-spiral engineering work without the comparison gate in
    [the test plan](TEST_PLAN.md). This release supplies **no native spiral or
    stationing bridge**. Bentley's managed Civil SDK is the documented route for a
    future ORD-native evaluator; see [API notes](API_NOTES.md).

    Safety limits: at most 10,000 output lines, 10,000 primitive components, 16 nested
    complex-string levels, and 1 billion master units for path length/numeric inputs.
    Spacing and line length must exceed four computational distance tolerances.
    The active model must be writable and the active level must permit modification
    and deletion, allowing cleanup after a failed write. No levels are unlocked.

    Ordinary `AddElement`/`Rewrite`/`RemoveElement` operations participate in
    MicroStation's normal Undo history. A single grouped Undo transaction is **not
    promised**. Verify the required number of Undo steps in your ORD build. Runtime
    write failures are handled by best-effort removal, not a database transaction;
    inspect any reported cleanup failure before retrying.

    ## Troubleshooting

    | Symptom | Action |
    | --- | --- |
    | `Variable not defined: frmPerpendicularLines` | Create and name the actual blank UserForm, then paste its code; `.bas` import is insufficient. |
    | `User-defined type not defined` / `MISSING` reference | Check Bentley MicroStation DGN and Forms references in the ORD VBA editor. Use ORD's supplied libraries. |
    | Macro cannot be found | Verify the loaded file and internal project name, then use the full module/procedure key-in above. Compile first. |
    | Form is blank in the designer | Expected: controls are constructed when the form runs. Paste the complete form code if it is blank at runtime. |
    | Unsupported native civil element/spiral | Use a verified standard geometry representation or an ORD Civil SDK tool; do not drop/stroke the alignment to hide the limitation. |
    | Corner, cusp, zero tangent, gap or ordering error | Inspect geometry; change the placement distances to avoid a corner, or select a continuous smooth path. No source repair is attempted. |
    | Curve distance/point validation fails | Stop and retain the failing test DGN/build details. Do not relax the tolerances merely to pass; check conversion and evaluator behavior in that ORD version. |
    | End distance rejected after reopening | The saved numeric end may exceed this alignment. Enter `End of Alignment` or a valid distance. |
    | First prompt asks for the alignment despite a preselection | Expected: the new workflow always locates the alignment, then picks start and end. |
    | Need settings before choosing the range | Reset during a range pick to open the dialog. Set Direction/length/placement, then use Pick Start/Pick End, or Full Alignment. |
    | End will not accept / preview prompt says end must follow start | Pick farther along in the current Direction, or Reset to change Direction and re-pick. |
    | Pick is equally close to different locations | Move away from a crossing/overlap or enter the desired geometry distance explicitly. |
    | Preview works but no lines appear in the element count | Expected until Generate Lines: dynamics never calls AddElement. |
    | START/END instructions appear at the lower left and native Tool Settings is empty | Expected: ShowPrompt uses the status bar. See API_NOTES.md for the separate modeless VBA panel and native .NET Tool Settings options; neither relocation is implemented yet. |
    | Switching commands closes the picker | Expected: this releases the source/session rather than reopening the dialog over the new command. Run the tool again. |
    | Too many lines / below model resolution | Increase spacing/line length or shorten the range. Review model working-unit resolution. |
    | Level/model is locked or read-only | Choose a writable active model and an unlocked writable active level; this macro does not change access permissions. |
    | Failure during write / rollback failure | Follow the message, inspect output, and use normal Undo as needed. Never assume a failed write was atomic. |

    checked for structural consistency. These are static checks, **not** a VBA compile
    or execution in ORD. The form, in-memory self-tests, DGN writes, symbology,
    selection callbacks, native civil representations, and Undo still require
    execution of [TEST_PLAN.md](TEST_PLAN.md) in ORD 2026. Record actual build numbers
    and results there or in a separate test record.

    The included user-built project's stored source was also compared with the four
    supplied source components; all matched after normalization. This read-only
    comparison does not compile the project or validate its runtime geometry.

    The user reported the original generator working after the naming/compile fixes.
    The newly added graphical range selection and dynamics have **not** been run
    inside ORD by the authoring agent; the new acceptance tests remain pending.
    The user subsequently reported graphical picking/preview working and supplied

    # Geometry and Bentley API notes

    ## Verification sources

    The primary VBA reference used during implementation was the help file delivered
    with the installed ORD 2026.00:

    ```text
    C:\Program Files\Bentley\OpenRoads Designer 2026.00\OpenRoadsDesigner\MicroStationVBA.chm
    ```

    Signatures and interface membership were also inspected by read-only reflection
    of its delivered `Assemblies\Bentley.Interop.MicroStationDGN.dll`. No ORD process
    was launched for that inspection. In the VBA Object Browser, use **F2** and search
    the Bentley library for the members below. In the help file, search their names.
    The HTML topic identifiers below make the reference repeatable after extracting
    the help with Windows HTML Help's `-decompile` option.

    | Member / topic | Use |
    | --- | --- |
    | `CommandState.StartLocate` and `ILocateCommandEvents` / `usobjilocatecommandevents.htm`, `ussmpLocate.htm` | Graphical locate, filtering, accept, reset and cleanup signatures |
    | `CommandState.StartPrimitive`, `IPrimitiveCommandEvents` / `usobjiprimitivecommandevents.htm`, `ussmpPrimitive.htm` | Start/end data-point callbacks, Reset, key-in and command cleanup signatures |
    | `CommandState.StartDynamics`, `EnableAccuSnap`; `Element.Redraw(DrawMode)` / `usmthstartdynamics.htm`, `usevtdynamics.htm` | Cursor-driven temporary cross-line previews; only Redraw inside Dynamics, never AddElement |
    | `Element.IsTraversableElement`, `AsTraversableElement` | Test/access traversable geometry |
    | `TraversableElement.PointAtDistance(Distance)` / `usmthpointatdistance.htm` | Original geometry endpoints for validation |
    | Typed `LineElement.Length`, `ArcElement.Length`, `BsplineCurveElement.Length`, `ComplexStringElement.Length` | Original element length; **`TraversableElement` does not have `Length`** |
    | `Element.AsVertexList.GetVertices` / `usmthgetvertices.htm` | Actual line string's zero-based vertices; there is no `AsLineStringElement` cast |
    | `ComplexStringElement.GetSubElements` / `usmthgetsubelements.htm` | Enumerate component copies without changing the source |
    | `BsplineCurve.FromElement(Element)` / `usmthfromelement.htm` | Convert supported line/arc/spline components to evaluable geometry objects |
    | `BsplineCurve.ComputeCurveLength([StartParameter, EndParameter, IgnoreZ])` / `usmthcomputecurvelength.htm` | Full/partial curve length; not chord length |
    | `BsplineCurve.EvaluatePointAtDistance(Parameter, DistanceFromStart, [StartParameter, Tolerance])` / `usmthevaluatepointatdistance.htm` | Return point and **output** curve parameter from a distance; explicit relative integration tolerance `1e-12` |
    | `BsplineCurve.EvaluatePointTangent(Tangent, Parameter)` / `usmthevaluatepointtangent.htm` | Return point and **output** unnormalized analytic tangent |
    | `BsplineCurve.ComputeMinimumDistance(ClosestPoint, ClosestParameter, FromPoint, Rotation, [Tolerance])` / `usmthcomputeminimumdistance.htm` | Closest point and **output** parameter; identity rotation computes XY distance, ignoring cursor Z |
    | `BsplineCurve.EvaluatePoint`, `Pole`, `PolesCount`, `Knot`, `KnotsCount`, `Closed` | Independent point check, horizontal plane and knot checks |
    | `Application.CreateLineElement2(Template, StartPoint, EndPoint)` / `usmthcreatelineelement2.htm` | Prepare independent straight output elements using active settings with `Template = Nothing` |
    | `Element.Level` / `usprolevel.htm` | A real `Level` object can be assigned only **after** insertion; newly created elements get active level automatically |
    | `ActiveSettings.Level`, `Color`, `LineStyle`, `LineWeight` | Capture settings on Generate; `LineStyle` is an object assigned with `Set` |
    | `ModelReference.AddElement`, `Element.Rewrite`, `ModelReference.RemoveElement` | Write and best-effort rollback |
    | `ModelReference.IsReadOnly`, `Level.ElementAccess` | Preflight writable output; `DesignFile.IsReadOnly` is not used |
    | `ModelReference.IsActive`, `IsAttachment`, `Type` | Check the captured model is current, reject attachments and exclude sheet models |
    | `ModelReference.MasterUnit`, `SubUnit`, `UORsPerMasterUnit`; `MeasurementUnit.Label` | Working units and computational resolution |

    The optional self-test also uses documented `CreateLineElement1`,
    `CreateArcElement2`, `CreateComplexStringElement1` with a `ChainableElement()`
    array, `CreateBsplineCurveElement1`, and `BsplineCurve.SetPoles/Order/SetKnots`.
    No deprecated distance-to-parameter method, MDL pointer/DLL declaration, C
    expression, guessed `PointAtStation` method, or Civil COM object is used.

    The load/run syntax follows Bentley's
    [Getting Started with Visual Basic](https://bentleysystems.service-now.com/community?id=kb_article_view&sysparm_article=KB0109946)
    and [Running a VBA Routine by Keyin](https://bentleysystems.service-now.com/community?id=kb_article_view&sysparm_article=KB0039735).

    ## Geometry policies

    These are **implementation safeguards, not roadway design criteria**:

    - Distance tolerance `T = max(2 / UORsPerMasterUnit, sourceLength * 1e-10)`, in
      master units. It accounts for two model resolution units plus an accumulated
      numerical length budget. It is used for endpoint matching,
      length residuals, and coincidence with range ends/joins. The code does not
      round placement distances to displayed precision.
    - All component poles must share the geometry origin's Z within
      `max(1e-12, abs(originZ)*1e-14)` master units, a floating-point plane-check
      budget. This is deliberately tighter than the distance tolerance so small
      slopes or accumulated component elevation changes are not mistaken for a
      horizontal alignment.
    - `EvaluatePointAtDistance` receives a **relative integration error request** of
      `1e-12`. Each result is checked by recomputing partial curve length from zero
      to the returned parameter. This is a cross-check within Bentley's curve engine,
      not an independent proof of the engine. The returned point is also compared
      with `EvaluatePointTangent`'s point.
    - B-spline parameter is **not** generally a fraction of length. The code never
      uses `distance / totalLength` as the parameter.
    - XY tangents are normalized and rotated left to `(-ty, tx)`. Centered endpoints
      are `p +/- length/2 * normal`. Left-only uses `p + length * normal` to `p`;
      right-only uses `p` to `p - length * normal`. All endpoints retain `p.Z`.
    - Components keep their stored order; a component is traversed backwards if its
      end matches the preceding endpoint. No gap is bridged and no source is reversed.
      Ordering that cannot be reconciled with the parent's endpoints is rejected.
    - At requested component joins, endpoint tangents must agree within a unit-vector
      difference of `1e-6` (approximately one microradian). A non-smooth corner has
      no unique mathematical tangent, so placement there fails the whole preflight.
    - Internal spline knots are checked on both sides using Bentley's derivatives;
      no numerical derivative or nearest-segment tangent is used for a curved
      component. Side probes use `1e-8` of the neighboring parameter span, with a
      further factor of `0.01` for point-limit checks. Very small spans, extreme
      curvature, or near-degenerate splines may be conservatively rejected. These
      probes are discontinuity guards, not a certification of a civil spiral fit.
    - Graphical picking calls `ComputeMinimumDistance` for every component with
      `Matrix3dIdentity`, checks the returned point against `EvaluatePoint`, and checks
      its reported distance against explicit XY distance. Cumulative path distance
      is the preceding components' lengths plus `ComputeCurveLength(0, parameter)`,
      with component/global reversal applied. It is never the distance from the
      cursor to an endpoint, a parameter fraction, or a snapped polyline surrogate.
    - All component candidates within `T` of the best XY distance are checked for
      conflicting chain distances. Shared endpoints with the same distance are
      allowed; crossings/overlaps with distinct distances are rejected. Bentley's
      method returns only one minimum per individual B-spline. Multiple equally
      close locations **within** one B-spline cannot be resolved by this interface;
      use unambiguous cursor positions or type a distance. Its optional tolerance
      argument is documented as **unused for B-splines**, so none is supplied and no
      tighter closest-point convergence is claimed from that parameter.
    - Dynamics draws the moving limit and, during end selection, the fixed start.
      It passes the event's `DrawMode` unchanged to `Redraw`. It creates only temporary
      in-memory lines and does not mutate the data-point argument or source geometry.
      Accepted picks stop the primitive command before resuming the modal form or
      next pick. Cleanup caused by another command releases the session without
      opening a modal dialog over that command.
    - Inputs/geometry below these budgets are rejected. Do not weaken safeguards to
      make an unexplained failure pass. Investigate the original geometry and ORD
      build against the analytical fixtures first.

    ## Status prompts and the native Tool Settings panel

    `ShowPrompt` and `ShowCommand` are documented to write to MicroStation's status
    area, explaining the lower-left range text. They do not populate Tool Settings.
    The installed ORD 2026 VBA `Application`/`CommandState` interfaces checked here
    expose no Tool Settings form-attachment API.

    Bentley's [Adding Windows Forms to Add-ins](https://bentleysystems.service-now.com/community?id=kb_article_view&sysparm_article=KB0012740)
    documents native Tool Settings integration through a managed form derived from
    `Bentley.MstnPlatformNET.WinForms.Adapter`, using
    `AttachToToolSettings(MyAddin.Addin)`. That is a .NET add-in interface, not a
    method available on this VBA `UserForm`.

    Bentley also documents [modeless VBA forms](https://bentleysystems.service-now.com/community?id=kb_article_view&sysparm_article=KB0028347).
    A floating modeless VBA panel could show the current step/distance near the
    right-hand tools while mouse picking continues. It would remain a separate
    window. Native panel integration needs the SDK add-in route. Neither UI change
    has been implemented; the choice is pending. Simply changing the existing
    modal `.Show` to `vbModeless` would require changing its session callbacks too.

    ## Native ORD geometry and the documented alternative

    Bentley documents the civil schema separately from basic DGN geometry in
    [Understanding Geometry model](https://docs.bentley.com/LiveContent/web/OpenRoads%20Designer%20SDK%20Help-v1/en/GUID-E99BC4FE-A0C9-4C20-8756-E8104911C083.html).
    The official examples obtain a managed `ConsensusConnection`, a
    `GeometricModel`, and its native `Alignment` objects. This supports the decision
    to keep native alignment/stationing work in an ORD-specific SDK layer rather
    than assuming VBA's ordinary DGN elements expose it.

    Bentley's [ORD SDK Developer Guide](https://bentleysystems.service-now.com/community?id=kb_article_view&sysparm_article=KB0012549)
    documents managed and unmanaged development, building examples, running custom
    projects inside ORD, and debugging managed projects. The
    [Civil Programming index](https://bentleysystems.service-now.com/community?id=kb_article_view&sysparm_article=KB0012546)
    links the SDK downloads, installation, examples and geometry-model documentation.

    For native spiral/station-equation support beyond the verified exposed VBA
    geometry, a **separate Bentley Civil SDK/.NET add-in is required by this
    implementation's support contract**. A future evaluator must read the native
    alignment's actual horizontal primitive definitions, use a validated native
    distance/tangent traversal, and separately translate displayed stationing and
    station equations. It must be built and tested against the installed ORD 2026
    SDK version. No claim is made here that one particular undocumented managed
    method or a generic curve conversion handles all native spirals.

    The absence of a spiral-specific evaluator in this VBA release does not prove
    that every native alignment is inaccessible. It means the tool cannot establish
    the native definition's accuracy through these ordinary VBA interfaces alone.
    If the selected element is a B-spline representation, the macro evaluates that
    **B-spline**, with no guarantee about its fit to an underlying clothoid. If it
    is a line string, it evaluates that **line string**, with no claim that its chord
    length equals the native spiral length. The manual support gate must establish
    equivalence before use for civil geometry.
