        import io
        import json
        import sys
        import tempfile
        import unittest
        from pathlib import Path
        from urllib.parse import parse_qs, urlparse
        sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1] / 'python'))
        from usgs_client import DATASETS, TNMClient, download, normalize


        class Response(io.BytesIO):
            def __init__(self, value, length=None):
                raw=value if isinstance(value,bytes) else json.dumps(value).encode()
                super().__init__(raw)
                self.headers={} if length is None else {'Content-Length': str(length)}


        def item(n=1):
            return {'sourceId': str(n), 'title': f'Tile {n}', 'sizeInBytes': 4,
                    'downloadURL': f'https://example.org/{n}.tif',
                    'publicationDate': '2024-01-01', 'boundingBox': {'minX':-89,'minY':33,'maxX':-88,'maxY':34}}


        class ClientTests(unittest.TestCase):
            def test_catalog_query_pagination_dedup(self):
                urls=[]
                def open_url(url):
                    urls.append(url)
                    if '/datasets' in url:
                        return Response([{'tags':[{'sbDatasetTag':DATASETS['dem1m']}]}])
                    offset=int(parse_qs(urlparse(url).query)['offset'][0])
                    return Response({'total':3,'items': [item(1), item(1)] if offset==0 else [item(2)],'errors':[]})
                products=TNMClient(opener=open_url).search('dem1m',[-88.9,33.1,-88.8,33.2])
                self.assertEqual([p['id'] for p in products],['1','2'])
                query=parse_qs(urlparse(urls[1]).query)
                self.assertEqual(query['datasets'],[DATASETS['dem1m']])
                self.assertEqual(query['prodFormats'],['GeoTIFF'])
                self.assertEqual(query['bbox'],['-88.9000000000,33.1000000000,-88.8000000000,33.2000000000'])
                self.assertEqual(parse_qs(urlparse(urls[2]).query)['offset'],['2'])

            def test_total_limit_missing_coverage_and_api_errors(self):
                for page in ({'total':0,'items':[]}, {'total':501,'items':[]},
                             {'total':1,'items':[]}, {'total':0,'items':[], 'errors':['outage']}):
                    def opener(url):
                        return Response([{'sbDatasetTag':DATASETS['dem13']}]) if '/datasets' in url else Response(page)
                    if page == {'total':0,'items':[]}:
                        self.assertEqual(TNMClient(opener).search('dem13',[-89,33,-88,34]),[])
                    else:
                        with self.assertRaises(ValueError): TNMClient(opener).search('dem13',[-89,33,-88,34])

            def test_repeated_page_guard(self):
                def opener(url):
                    return Response([{'sbDatasetTag':DATASETS['dem1m']}]) if '/datasets' in url else Response({'total':3,'items':[item()]})
                with self.assertRaisesRegex(ValueError,'repeated'): TNMClient(opener).search('dem1m',[-89,33,-88,34])

            def test_product_url_and_metadata_validation(self):
                p=item(); p['downloadURL']='http://example.org/1.tif'
                with self.assertRaises(ValueError): normalize(p,'dem1m')
                p=item(); p['boundingBox']=None
                with self.assertRaises(ValueError): normalize(p,'dem1m')
                p=item(); p['downloadURL']='https://example.org/1.zip'
                with self.assertRaises(ValueError): normalize(p,'dem1m')
                p=item(); p['downloadURL']='https://example.org/1.laz'
                self.assertEqual(normalize(p,'lidar')['kind'],'lidar')

            def test_download_receipt_reuse_and_corruption(self):
                with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as root:
                    p=normalize(item(),'dem1m')
                    path, receipt=download(p,root,10,opener=lambda u:Response(b'data',4))
                    self.assertEqual(path.read_bytes(),b'data')
                    self.assertEqual(receipt['bytes'],4)
                    path2,_=download(p,root,10,opener=lambda u:self.fail('cache should be reused'))
                    self.assertEqual(path2,path)
                    path.write_bytes(b'bad!')
                    with self.assertRaises(ValueError): download(p,root,10)

            def test_incomplete_capped_cancelled_transfers_leave_no_partial(self):
                for length,budget in ((20,100),(4,3),(None,3)):
                    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as root:
                        with self.assertRaises(ValueError):
                            download(normalize(item(),'dem1m'),root,budget,opener=lambda u:Response(b'data',length))
                        self.assertEqual(list(Path(root).iterdir()),[])
                with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as root:
                    def cancel(): raise InterruptedError('cancel')
                    with self.assertRaises(InterruptedError):
                        download(normalize(item(),'dem1m'),root,10,check=cancel,opener=lambda u:Response(b'data',4))
                    self.assertEqual(list(Path(root).iterdir()),[])
        PY
        cat > USGSTerrainDownloader/tests/test_processing.py <<'PY'
        import json
        import sys
        import tempfile
        import unittest
        from pathlib import Path
        sys.path.insert(0,str(Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]/'python'))
        from crs_utils import prepare_boundary
        from terrain_processor import process_dem, inspect_raster
        try:
            from osgeo import gdal, osr
            import numpy as np
            HAS_GDAL=True
        except ImportError:
            HAS_GDAL=False


        @unittest.skipUnless(HAS_GDAL,'GDAL unavailable: run with QGIS/OSGeo4W Python')
        class ProcessingTests(unittest.TestCase):
            def setUp(self):
                self.temp=tempfile.TemporaryDirectory(); self.root=Path(self.temp.name)
                self.confirm={'unit':'m','crs':'EPSG:5703','evidence':'Synthetic fixture declares NAVD88; not real survey data.'}
                self.config={'crs_confirmed':True,'coordinates_are_grid':True,'source_crs':'EPSG:26916','master_unit':'m',
                             'boundary':[[300000,3700000],[300020,3700000],[300020,3700020],[300000,3700020]],
                             'source_vertical':self.confirm,'resolution':10,'elevation_unit':'us-ft','clip':True}
                self.boundary=prepare_boundary(self.config)
                self.product={'id':'fixture','title':'Synthetic DEM'}

            def tearDown(self): self.temp.cleanup()

            def raster(self,name='a.tif', x=300000, y=3700020, values=None, crs=26916, unit='m', nodata=-9999, pixel=10):
                if values is None: values=np.full((2,2),100,dtype=float)
                path=self.root/name
                ds=gdal.GetDriverByName('GTiff').Create(str(path),values.shape[1],values.shape[0],1,gdal.GDT_Float64)
                ds.SetGeoTransform((x,pixel,0,y,0,-pixel))
                if crs:
                    srs=osr.SpatialReference(); srs.ImportFromEPSG(crs); ds.SetProjection(srs.ExportToWkt())
                band=ds.GetRasterBand(1); band.WriteArray(values); band.SetNoDataValue(nodata); band.SetUnitType(unit)
                band=None; ds=None
                return path

            def process(self,paths,config=None,boundary=None):
                folder=self.root/'output'; folder.mkdir(exist_ok=True)
                products=[dict(self.product,id=str(i)) for i in range(len(paths))]
                return process_dem(paths,products,config or self.config,boundary or self.boundary,folder)

            def test_constant_dem_units_crs_and_readback(self):
                source=self.raster()
                report=self.process([source])
                ds=gdal.Open(report['output'])
                self.assertTrue(np.allclose(ds.ReadAsArray(),328.0833333333333,atol=1e-9))
                self.assertEqual(ds.GetRasterBand(1).GetUnitType(),'us-ft')
                self.assertEqual(report['gap_cells'],0)
                self.assertEqual(report['valid_boundary_cells'],4)
                self.assertEqual(report['elevation_multiplier'],3937/1200)
                self.assertIn('North American Vertical Datum', report['vertical_datum'])
                self.assertEqual(gdal.Open(str(source)).GetRasterBand(1).ReadAsArray()[0,0],100)

            def test_horizontal_reprojection_does_not_scale_z(self):
                path=self.raster('utm.tif',x=299900,y=3700100,values=np.full((40,40),123.45))
                from pyproj import Transformer
                t=Transformer.from_crs(26916,2254,always_xy=True)
                c=dict(self.config,source_crs='EPSG:2254',master_unit='us-ft',elevation_unit='m',resolution=20)
                c['boundary']=[list(t.transform(x,y)) for x,y in self.config['boundary']]
                b=prepare_boundary(c)
                result=self.process([path],c,b)
                a=gdal.Open(result['output']).ReadAsArray()
                self.assertTrue(np.allclose(a[a!=-1e300],123.45,atol=1e-8))
                self.assertEqual(result['elevation_multiplier'],1)

            def test_compatible_tiles_mosaic(self):
                a=self.raster(); b=self.raster('b.tif',x=300020)
                c=dict(self.config,boundary=[[300000,3700000],[300040,3700000],[300040,3700020],[300000,3700020]])
                result=self.process([a,b],c,prepare_boundary(c))
                self.assertEqual(result['valid_boundary_cells'],8)

            def test_gap_rejected_and_original_retained(self):
                a=self.raster(values=np.array([[100.,-9999],[100.,100.]]))
                with self.assertRaisesRegex(ValueError,'Coverage gap'): self.process([a])
                self.assertTrue(a.exists()); self.assertFalse((self.root/'output/terrain.tif').exists())
                self.assertFalse((self.root/'output/terrain.partial.tif').exists())

            def test_gap_can_be_explicitly_retained(self):
                a=self.raster(values=np.array([[100.,-9999],[100.,100.]]))
                c=dict(self.config,allow_gaps=True)
                result=self.process([a],c)
                self.assertEqual(result['gap_cells'],1)

            def test_missing_crs_unit_conflict_unknown_vertical(self):
                for kwargs,confirmation in (({'crs':None},self.confirm),({'unit':'us-ft'},self.confirm),({},None)):
                    path=self.raster('invalid.tif',**kwargs)
                    with self.assertRaises((ValueError,TypeError)): inspect_raster(path,confirmation)

            def test_vertical_datum_mixing_rejected(self):
                a=self.raster(); b=self.raster('b.tif',x=300020)
                c=dict(self.config,vertical_confirmations={'0':self.confirm,'1':dict(self.confirm,crs='EPSG:5714')})
                with self.assertRaisesRegex(ValueError,'Mixed vertical'): self.process([a,b],c)

            def test_overlaps_and_resolution_conflict(self):
                a=self.raster(); b=self.raster('b.tif')
                with self.assertRaisesRegex(ValueError,'overlap'): self.process([a,b])
                b=self.raster('b.tif',pixel=5)
                with self.assertRaisesRegex(ValueError,'resolutions'): self.process([a,b])

            def test_cancel_and_output_cap(self):
                a=self.raster(); folder=self.root/'cancelled'; folder.mkdir()
                def cancel(): raise InterruptedError('cancel')
                with self.assertRaises(InterruptedError): process_dem([a],[self.product],self.config,self.boundary,folder,check=cancel)
                c=dict(self.config,resolution=0.0001)
                with self.assertRaisesRegex(ValueError,'100 million'): self.process([a],c)

            def test_invalid_polygon_rejected(self):
                a=self.raster()
                b=dict(self.boundary,ring=[[300000,3700000],[300020,3700020],[300000,3700020],[300020,3700000],[300000,3700000]])
                with self.assertRaisesRegex(ValueError,'self-intersecting'): self.process([a],boundary=b)
        PY

## Assistant

The first raster tests exposed a metadata issue: GDAL did not retain the foot-based vertical units in the output CRS as intended. The read-back check caught it and blocked the output. I’m fixing that encoding and adding checks for both foot definitions.

    """Explicit model-master -> CRS-native XY -> geographic boundary contract."""
    import math

    from pyproj import CRS, Geod, Transformer, network

    network.set_network_enabled(False)
    UNIT_METERS = {"m": 1.0, "ft": 0.3048, "us-ft": 1200 / 3937}
    MAX_VERTICES = 20000


    def require(condition, message):
        if not condition:
            raise ValueError(message)


    def linear_factor(source, target):
        require(source in UNIT_METERS and target in UNIT_METERS,
                "Units must be m, ft (international), or us-ft (US survey).")
        return UNIT_METERS[source] / UNIT_METERS[target]


    def horizontal_crs(value):
        crs = CRS.from_user_input(value)
        require(crs.is_projected and len(crs.axis_info) == 2,
                "Provide a two-dimensional projected horizontal CRS; no compound, vertical, or geographic DGN CRS.")
        require(all(a.direction in ("east", "north") for a in crs.axis_info),
                "Only east/north projected coordinate axes are supported.")
        factors = [a.unit_conversion_factor for a in crs.axis_info]
        require(abs(factors[0] - factors[1]) < 1e-14, "Mixed horizontal axis units are unsupported.")
        return crs


    def transformer(source, target):
        # Fail if PROJ's best operation needs missing grids; never allow ballpark substitution.
        return Transformer.from_crs(source, target, always_xy=True,
                                   allow_ballpark=False, only_best=True)


    def ring_valid(points):
        require(isinstance(points, list) and 3 <= len(points) <= MAX_VERTICES,
                "Boundary needs 3 to 20,000 XY vertices.")
        ring = []
        for point in points:
            require(len(point) == 2, "Boundary vertices must contain XY only.")
            p = [float(v) for v in point]
            require(all(math.isfinite(v) for v in p), "Nonfinite boundary coordinate.")
            ring.append(p)
        if ring[0] != ring[-1]:
            ring.append(ring[0][:])
        require(len(ring) >= 4, "Boundary has fewer than three distinct corners.")
        require(all(a != b for a, b in zip(ring, ring[1:])), "Duplicate adjacent boundary vertices.")
        # Translate before shoelace to avoid cancellation at large State Plane coordinates.
        x0, y0 = ring[0]
        area = sum((a[0]-x0)*(b[1]-y0)-(b[0]-x0)*(a[1]-y0)
                   for a, b in zip(ring, ring[1:])) / 2
        require(abs(area) > 1e-10, "Boundary has zero XY area.")
        return ring


    def densify(ring, native_to_meters, segment_m=100):
        result = []
        for a, b in zip(ring, ring[1:]):
            n = max(1, math.ceil(math.dist(a, b) * native_to_meters / segment_m))
            require(len(result) + n < MAX_VERTICES, "Boundary too large/complex; reduce the area.")
            result.extend([[a[0]+(b[0]-a[0])*i/n, a[1]+(b[1]-a[1])*i/n]
                           for i in range(n)])
        result.append(result[0][:])
        return result


    def prepare_boundary(config):
        require(config.get("crs_confirmed") is True,
                "Confirm source CRS, master-unit type, and absence of local/ground transforms.")
        require(config.get("coordinates_are_grid") is True,
                "Local offsets, rotation, ground scale, or custom Bentley GCS modifiers require a validated adapter.")
        crs = horizontal_crs(config["source_crs"])
        unit = config.get("master_unit")
        require(unit in UNIT_METERS, "Explicit model master unit required: m, ft, or us-ft.")
        native_factor = crs.axis_info[0].unit_conversion_factor
        scale = UNIT_METERS[unit] / native_factor
        ring = ring_valid(config["boundary"])
        ring = [[x*scale, y*scale] for x, y in ring]
        dense = densify(ring, native_factor)
        operation = transformer(crs, "EPSG:4326")
        xs, ys = operation.transform(*zip(*dense), errcheck=True)
        require(all(math.isfinite(x) and math.isfinite(y) and -180 <= x <= 180 and -90 < y < 90
                    for x, y in zip(xs, ys)), "Transformed boundary is not valid longitude/latitude.")
        bbox = [min(xs)-1e-7, min(ys)-1e-7, max(xs)+1e-7, max(ys)+1e-7]
        require(bbox[2]-bbox[0] <= 2 and bbox[3]-bbox[1] <= 2,
                "Extent exceeds the 2-degree safety limit (antimeridian areas unsupported).")
        area, _ = Geod(ellps="WGS84").polygon_area_perimeter(xs, ys)
        require(0 < abs(area) <= 1e10, "Geographic area must be positive and <= 10,000 square km.")
        bounds = crs.area_of_use
        if bounds:
            require(all(bounds.west-0.01 <= x <= bounds.east+0.01 and
                        bounds.south-0.01 <= y <= bounds.north+0.01 for x, y in zip(xs, ys)),
                    "Boundary lies outside the CRS area of use. Verify CRS and model coordinates.")
        return {"crs_wkt": crs.to_wkt(), "crs_name": crs.name,
                "horizontal_unit": crs.axis_info[0].unit_name,
                "model_to_native_scale": scale, "ring": dense, "bbox": bbox,
                "area_m2": abs(area), "search_operation": operation.description,
                "search_operation_accuracy_m": operation.accuracy,
                "search_pipeline": operation.get_last_used_operation().definition}
                    creationOptions=["TILED=YES", "COMPRESS=DEFLATE", "BIGTIFF=IF_SAFER", "GEOTIFF_VERSION=1.1"]))
            except RuntimeError:
                if callback_error:
                    raise callback_error[0]
                raise
            require(warped is not None, "GDAL warp produced no output.")
            # Rasterize expected coverage separately: NoData inside the polygon is a coverage gap.
            mask = gdal.GetDriverByName("MEM").Create("", warped.RasterXSize, warped.RasterYSize, 1, gdal.GDT_Byte)
            mask.SetGeoTransform(warped.GetGeoTransform()); mask.SetProjection(target.to_wkt())
            gdal.RasterizeLayer(mask, [1], layer, burn_values=[1])
            band = warped.GetRasterBand(1)
            valid_count = expected = gaps = 0
            minimum, maximum = math.inf, -math.inf
            for y in range(0, warped.RasterYSize, 256):
                for x in range(0, warped.RasterXSize, 256):
                    check()
                    w, h = min(256, warped.RasterXSize-x), min(256, warped.RasterYSize-y)
                    a = band.ReadAsArray(x, y, w, h)
                    inside = mask.GetRasterBand(1).ReadAsArray(x, y, w, h) == 1
                    valid = a != nodata
                    require(np.isfinite(a[valid]).all(), "Nonfinite raster elevation detected.")
                    expected += int(inside.sum()); gaps += int((inside & ~valid).sum())
                    # Float64 conversion applies only to valid samples, independently of XY warp.
                    a[valid] *= factor
                    require(np.isfinite(a[valid]).all() and not np.any(a[valid] == nodata), "Elevation conversion overflow/NoData collision.")
                    samples = a[valid & inside]
                    if samples.size:
                        minimum = min(minimum, float(samples.min())); maximum = max(maximum, float(samples.max()))
                        valid_count += int(samples.size)
                    band.WriteArray(a, x, y)
                progress(f"Processing: elevation units / coverage {min(y+256, warped.RasterYSize)}/{warped.RasterYSize} rows")
            require(expected > 0 and valid_count > 0, "No valid DEM cell centers inside boundary. Reduce cell size or verify coverage.")
            require(gaps == 0 or config.get("allow_gaps") is True,
                    f"Coverage gap: {gaps}/{expected} boundary cells are NoData. Select more coverage; output not released.")
            vertical = output_vertical(datums[0], output_unit)
            compound = CompoundCRS("DGN horizontal + verified source vertical (no datum shift)", [target, vertical])
            output_srs = osr.SpatialReference()
            output_srs.ImportFromWkt(compound.to_wkt())
            # GDAL GeoTIFF needs the vertical UNIT authority to retain non-metre units.
            output_srs.SetAuthority("COMPD_CS|VERT_CS|UNIT", "EPSG",
                                    {"m": 9001, "ft": 9002, "us-ft": 9003}[output_unit])
            warped.SetSpatialRef(output_srs)
            band.SetUnitType(output_unit)
            warped.SetMetadataItem("VERTICAL_DATUM", datums[0].datum.name)
            warped.SetMetadataItem("ELEVATION_UNIT", output_unit)
            warped.SetMetadataItem("VERTICAL_OPERATION", "Unit scaling only; no vertical datum transformation")
            warped.SetMetadataItem("SOURCE_REPORT", "report.json")
            band.FlushCache(); warped.FlushCache(); band = None; warped = None; mask = None; vrt = None
            verify = gdal.Open(str(temp))
            require(verify is not None and CRS.from_wkt(verify.GetProjection()).equals(compound), "Output CRS verification failed.")
            require(verify.GetRasterBand(1).GetUnitType() == output_unit, "Output elevation unit tag verification failed.")
            require(verify.GetRasterBand(1).Checksum() >= 0, "Output read-back checksum failed.")
            geotransform = list(verify.GetGeoTransform())
            verify = None
            check()
            temp.replace(output)
            return {"output": str(output), "sources": infos, "output_crs_wkt": compound.to_wkt(),
                    "horizontal_pipeline": operation.definition, "horizontal_accuracy_m": operation.accuracy,
                    "vertical_datum": datums[0].datum.name, "vertical_unit": output_unit,
                    "elevation_multiplier": factor, "vertical_transformation": "none; unit conversion only",
                    "resolution": resolution, "resampling": "bilinear", "geotransform": geotransform,
                    "valid_boundary_cells": valid_count, "expected_boundary_cells": expected,
                    "gap_cells": gaps, "overlaps": overlaps, "overlap_policy": "selected list order, last wins",
                    "elevation_range": [minimum, maximum], "gdal_version": gdal.VersionInfo("--version"),
                    "warning": "Not survey-grade. No vertical datum shift performed. Validate with project control before design use."}
        finally:
            verify = band = warped = mask = vrt = None
            # A failed/cancelled raster is never named terrain.tif.
            temp.unlink(missing_ok=True)


    def inspect_lidar(path, executable, check=lambda: None):
        """Optional PDAL metadata/schema only. No ground classification or rasterization implied."""
        require(Path(executable).is_file(), "Configure a full PDAL executable path for point-cloud inspection.")
        check()
        result = subprocess.run([str(executable), "info", "--metadata", "--schema", str(path)],
                                capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=180, check=True)
        check()
        return json.loads(result.stdout)

        import json
        import sys
        import tempfile
        import unittest
        from pathlib import Path
        from unittest.mock import patch
        sys.path.insert(0,str(Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]/'python'))
        from main import Job, run, ui_file
        from test_crs import request
        from test_usgs import item
        from usgs_client import normalize
        try:
            from osgeo import gdal
            HAS_GDAL=True
        except ImportError:
            HAS_GDAL=False


        class JobTests(unittest.TestCase):
            def test_search_manifest_ui_and_stale_boundary(self):
                with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as root:
                    folder=Path(root)
                    config=dict(request(),action='search',dataset='dem1m')
                    with patch('main.TNMClient') as client:
                        client.return_value.search.return_value=[normalize(item(),'dem1m')]
                        result=run(config,Job(folder))
                    self.assertEqual(result['products'][0]['id'],'1')
                    self.assertEqual((folder/'products.tsv').read_bytes()[:2],b'\xff\xfe')
                    self.assertIn('search_complete',(folder/'status.txt').read_text(encoding='utf-16'))
                    config.update(action='process',selected_ids=['1'],search_manifest=str(folder/'search.json'))
                    config['boundary'][0][0]+=1
                    with self.assertRaisesRegex(ValueError,'changed since search'): run(config,Job(folder))

            def test_cooperative_cancel_and_utf16(self):
                with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as root:
                    folder=Path(root); (folder/'cancel.flag').touch()
                    with self.assertRaises(InterruptedError): Job(folder).check()
                    ui_file(folder/'unicode.tsv',['Δ\t工程\tUS survey feet'])
                    self.assertIn('工程',(folder/'unicode.tsv').read_text(encoding='utf-16'))

            def test_unknown_size_needs_confirmation_before_transfer(self):
                with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as root:
                    folder=Path(root)
                    c=dict(request(),action='search',dataset='dem1m')
                    p=normalize(item(),'dem1m');p['bytes']=None
                    with patch('main.TNMClient') as client:
                        client.return_value.search.return_value=[p]; run(c,Job(folder))
                    c.update(action='process',selected_ids=['1'],search_manifest=str(folder/'search.json'))
                    with patch('main.download') as transfer:
                        with self.assertRaisesRegex(ValueError,'unknown size'): run(c,Job(folder))
                        transfer.assert_not_called()

            @unittest.skipUnless(HAS_GDAL,'GDAL unavailable')
            def test_full_job_and_failure_report_with_tiny_raster(self):
                from test_processing import ProcessingTests
                helper=ProcessingTests(); helper.setUp()
                try:
                    source=helper.raster()
                    c=dict(helper.config,schema_version=1,action='search',dataset='dem1m')
                    search=helper.root/'search'; search.mkdir()
                    p=normalize(item(),'dem1m')
                    with patch('main.TNMClient') as client:
                        client.return_value.search.return_value=[p];run(c,Job(search))
                    c.update(action='process',selected_ids=['1'],search_manifest=str(search/'search.json'),max_download_bytes=1000000)
                    dest=helper.root/'process';dest.mkdir()
                    with patch('main.download',return_value=(source,{'url':p['url'],'bytes':source.stat().st_size,'sha256':'fixture'})):
                        report=run(c,Job(dest))
                    self.assertEqual(report['state'],'dem_complete')
                    self.assertTrue((dest/'terrain.tif').exists())
                    self.assertEqual(json.loads((dest/'report.json').read_text())['processing']['gap_cells'],0)
                    fail=helper.root/'fail';fail.mkdir()
                    with patch('main.download',side_effect=OSError('network interrupted')):
                        with self.assertRaises(OSError):run(c,Job(fail))
                    self.assertEqual(json.loads((fail/'report.json').read_text())['state'],'failed')
                    self.assertFalse((fail/'terrain.tif').exists())
                finally:
                    helper.tearDown()

        """Run in a working QGIS/OSGeo4W shell; capture only GIS paths for the child worker."""
        import json
        import os
        import sys
        from pathlib import Path


        def capture(folder):
            folder = Path(folder)
            if not folder.is_dir():
                raise ValueError('Choose an existing configuration folder.')
            # Verify before writing a configuration; this interpreter must support the DEM pipeline.
            from osgeo import gdal
            import numpy
            import pyproj
            if int(gdal.VersionInfo()) < 3080000:
                raise ValueError('GDAL >= 3.8 required')
            values = {k: os.environ[k] for k in ('PYTHONHOME','PYTHONPATH','GDAL_DATA','PROJ_DATA','SSL_CERT_FILE') if os.environ.get(k)}
            values['PATH_prepend'] = os.environ.get('PATH','')
            if 'PROJ_DATA' not in values:
                values['PROJ_DATA'] = os.environ.get('PROJ_LIB') or pyproj.datadir.get_data_dir()
            if 'GDAL_DATA' not in values:
                path = gdal.FindFile('gdal', 'gdalvrt.xsd')
                if path:
                    values['GDAL_DATA'] = str(Path(path).parent)
            (folder/'runtime-environment.json').write_text(json.dumps(values,indent=2),encoding='utf-8')
            (folder/'runtime-python.txt').write_text(sys.executable,encoding='utf-8')
            return {'python': sys.executable, 'gdal': gdal.VersionInfo('--version'),
                    'pyproj': pyproj.__version__, 'numpy': numpy.__version__}


        if __name__ == '__main__':
            if len(sys.argv) != 2:
                sys.exit('Usage: python capture_environment.py existing-config-folder')
            print(json.dumps(capture(sys.argv[1]),indent=2))
        PY

        Public Function TerrainDefaultDestination() As String
            Dim fs As Object, folder As String, stream As Object, probe As String
            On Error GoTo LocalFallback
            Set fs = CreateObject("Scripting.FileSystemObject")
            folder = fs.BuildPath(ActiveDesignFile.path, "USGS_Terrain")
            If Not fs.FolderExists(folder) Then fs.CreateFolder folder
            probe = fs.BuildPath(folder, fs.GetTempName)
            Set stream = fs.CreateTextFile(probe, False): stream.Close
            fs.DeleteFile probe
            TerrainDefaultDestination = folder
            Exit Function
        LocalFallback:
            On Error GoTo Failed
            Set fs = CreateObject("Scripting.FileSystemObject")
            folder = Environ$("LOCALAPPDATA") & "\USGSTerrainDownloader"
            If Not fs.FolderExists(folder) Then fs.CreateFolder folder
            TerrainDefaultDestination = folder
            Exit Function
        Failed:
            TerrainDefaultDestination = Environ$("TEMP")
        End Function

    # __init__ for osgeo package.

    # making the osgeo package version the same as the gdal version:
    from sys import platform, version_info
    if version_info >= (3, 8, 0) and platform == 'win32':
        import os
        if 'USE_PATH_FOR_GDAL_PYTHON' in os.environ and 'PATH' in os.environ:
            for p in os.environ['PATH'].split(';'):
                if p:
                    try:
                        os.add_dll_directory(p)
                    except (FileNotFoundError, OSError):
                        continue
        elif 'PATH' in os.environ:
            import glob
            for p in os.environ['PATH'].split(';'):
                try:
                    if glob.glob(os.path.join(p, 'gdal*.dll')) or glob.glob(os.path.join(p, 'libgdal*.dll')):
                        os.add_dll_directory(p)
                        break
                except (FileNotFoundError, OSError):
                    continue


    def swig_import_helper():
        import importlib
        from os.path import dirname, basename
        mname = basename(dirname(__file__)) + '._gdal'
        try:
            return importlib.import_module(mname)
        except ImportError:
            return importlib.import_module('_gdal')


    _gdal = swig_import_helper()
    del swig_import_helper

    __version__ = _gdal.__version__ = _gdal.VersionInfo("RELEASE_NAME")

    gdal_version = tuple(int(s) for s in str(__version__).split('.') if s.isdigit())[:3]
    python_version = tuple(version_info)[:3]

    # Setting this flag to True will cause importing osgeo to fail on an unsupported Python version.
    # Otherwise a deprecation warning will be issued instead.
    # Importing osgeo from an unsupported Python version might still partially work
    # because the core of GDAL Python bindings might still support an older Python version.
    # Hence the default option to just issue a warning.
    # To get complete functionality upgrading to the minimum supported version is needed.
    fail_on_unsupported_version = False

    # The following is a Sequence of tuples in the form of (gdal_version, python_version).
    # Each line represents the minimum supported Python version of a given GDAL version.
    # Introducing a new line for the next GDAL version will trigger a deprecation warning
    # when importing osgeo from a Python version which will not be
    # supported in the next version of GDAL.
    gdal_version_and_min_supported_python_version = (
        ((0, 0), (0, 0)),
        ((1, 0), (2, 0)),
        ((2, 0), (2, 7)),
        ((3, 3), (3, 6)),
        # ((3, 4), (3, 7)),
        # ((3, 5), (3, 8)),
    )


    def ver_str(ver):
        return '.'.join(str(v) for v in ver) if ver is not None else None


    minimum_supported_python_version_for_this_gdal_version = None
    this_python_version_will_be_deprecated_in_gdal_version = None
    last_gdal_version_to_supported_your_python_version = None
    next_version_of_gdal_will_use_python_version = None
    for gdal_ver, py_ver in gdal_version_and_min_supported_python_version:
        if gdal_version >= gdal_ver:
            minimum_supported_python_version_for_this_gdal_version = py_ver
        if python_version >= py_ver:
            last_gdal_version_to_supported_your_python_version = gdal_ver
        if not this_python_version_will_be_deprecated_in_gdal_version:
            if python_version < py_ver:
                this_python_version_will_be_deprecated_in_gdal_version = gdal_ver
                next_version_of_gdal_will_use_python_version = py_ver


    if python_version < minimum_supported_python_version_for_this_gdal_version:
        msg = 'Your Python version is {}, which is no longer supported by GDAL {}. ' \
              'Please upgrade your Python version to Python >= {}, ' \
              'or use GDAL <= {}, which supports your Python version.'.\
            format(ver_str(python_version), ver_str(gdal_version),
                   ver_str(minimum_supported_python_version_for_this_gdal_version),
                   ver_str(last_gdal_version_to_supported_your_python_version))

        if fail_on_unsupported_version:
            raise Exception(msg)
        else:
            from warnings import warn
            warn(msg, DeprecationWarning)
    elif this_python_version_will_be_deprecated_in_gdal_version:
        msg = 'You are using Python {} with GDAL {}. ' \
              'This Python version will be deprecated in GDAL {}. ' \

        $root = Join-Path $env:TEMP ('terrain bridge Unicode ' + [char]0x0394 + ' & test-' + [guid]::NewGuid())
        New-Item -ItemType Directory -Path $root > $null
        try {
            foreach ($case in @('invalid-executable','invalid-request')) {
                $folder = Join-Path $root $case
                New-Item -ItemType Directory -Path $folder > $null
                $request = Join-Path $folder 'request.json'
                $exe = if ($case -eq 'invalid-executable') { Join-Path $root 'missing.exe' } else { $Python }
                @{ schema_version=0; action='search'; python_executable=$exe } | ConvertTo-Json | Set-Content -LiteralPath $request -Encoding UTF8
                & powershell.exe -NoProfile -NonInteractive -File (Join-Path $source 'launch_worker.ps1') -Request $request
                if ($LASTEXITCODE -eq 0) { throw "Invalid input unexpectedly succeeded: $case" }
                $status = [IO.File]::ReadAllText((Join-Path $folder 'status.txt'), [Text.Encoding]::Unicode)
                if (-not $status.StartsWith('failed')) { throw "Missing failure status: $case" }
                if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath (Join-Path $folder 'error.log'))) { throw "Missing diagnostic log: $case" }
            }
            Write-Output 'PASS: Windows launcher handles spaces, Unicode, ampersands, missing interpreter, and worker failure.'
        } finally {
            Remove-Item -LiteralPath $root -Recurse -Force
        }

What is superelevation windows

## Assistant

`superelevation-windows` is the SSH shortcut for your Windows test PC, defined in the local instructions you supplied. I used it to test the downloader’s Windows launcher and Python code. Those checks didn’t open or modify any DGN files. ORD isn’t installed there, so they don’t count as ORD validation.

        ElseIf attempted > 0 Then
            originalError = originalError & vbCrLf & "This run's added elements were removed."

    End Function
    Private Function ReadSettings(first As Double, last As Double, spacing As Double, totalLength As Double, _
            distances() As Double) As Long
        spacing = ReadNumber(txtSpacing, "Line Spacing")
        totalLength = ReadNumber(txtLength, "Total Line Length")
        PLGRequire totalLength > 4# * PLGDistanceTolerance(), "Total Line Length must be positive and exceed four distance tolerances."
        first = ReadNumber(txtStart, "Start Distance")
        If Len(Trim$(txtEnd.Text)) = 0 Or LCase$(Trim$(txtEnd.Text)) = "end of alignment" Then
            last = PLGPathLength()
        Else
            last = ReadNumber(txtEnd, "End Distance")
        End If
        PLGRequire cboPlacement.ListIndex >= 0 And cboDirection.ListIndex >= 0, "Choose placement and direction."
        ReadSettings = PLGBuildDistances(first, last, spacing, CBool(chkStart.value), CBool(chkEnd.value), distances)
    End Function

    Private Sub RefreshEstimate()
        Dim first As Double, last As Double, spacing As Double, totalLength As Double, distances() As Double, n As Long
        If mInitializing Then Exit Sub
        On Error GoTo Invalid
        n = ReadSettings(first, last, spacing, totalLength, distances)
        lblEstimate.caption = "Estimated lines: " & CStr(n) & " | An optional ending line can shorten the final interval."
        cmdGenerate.Enabled = True
        Exit Sub
    Invalid:
        lblEstimate.caption = Err.Description
        cmdGenerate.Enabled = False
    End Sub

    Private Sub cmdGenerate_Click()
        Dim first As Double, last As Double, spacing As Double, totalLength As Double, distances() As Double
        Dim n As Long, result As String
        On Error GoTo Failed
        n = ReadSettings(first, last, spacing, totalLength, distances)
        cmdGenerate.Enabled = False
        result = PLGGenerate(first, last, spacing, totalLength, cboPlacement.ListIndex, _
            cboDirection.ListIndex = 1, CBool(chkStart.value), CBool(chkEnd.value))
        MsgBox result, vbInformation, "Perpendicular Line Generator"
        cmdGenerate.Enabled = True
        Me.Hide
        Exit Sub
    Failed:
        MsgBox Err.Description, vbExclamation, "Perpendicular Line Generator"
        RefreshEstimate
    End Sub

    Private Sub cmdCancel_Click()
        Me.Hide
    End Sub

    Private Sub UserForm_QueryClose(Cancel As Integer, CloseMode As Integer)
        If CloseMode = vbFormControlMenu Then
            Cancel = True
            Me.Hide
        End If
    End Sub

    Private Sub txtSpacing_Change()
        RefreshEstimate
    End Sub
    Private Sub txtLength_Change()
        RefreshEstimate
    End Sub
    Private Sub txtStart_Change()
        RefreshEstimate
    End Sub
    Private Sub txtEnd_Change()
        RefreshEstimate
    End Sub
    Private Sub cboPlacement_Change()
        RefreshEstimate
    End Sub
    Private Sub cboDirection_Change()
        RefreshEstimate
    End Sub
    Private Sub chkStart_Click()
        RefreshEstimate
    End Sub
    Private Sub chkEnd_Click()
        RefreshEstimate
    End Sub
    # ORD 2026 repeatable acceptance tests

    **Status: not executed inside ORD during implementation.** Static signature and
    source checks do not substitute for these tests. Compile and run in a disposable
    DGN/project first. Record ORD's full build, model dimensionality, working-unit
    definition, UORs per master unit, tolerance, and measured results. Do not promote
    native spiral support merely because output looks perpendicular on screen.

    ## Preparation and evidence

    1. Assemble/import as described in [README.md](README.md), then use **Debug >
       Compile**. Record compiler success/failure. Run the optional
       `modPerpendicularTests.RunSelfTests` with the generator/picker inactive. It creates
       only in-memory fixtures, verifies analytical lines/arcs/B-splines, cumulative
       joins, both directions, placements, counts, and rejection cases. It does not
       exercise AddElement, form layout, selection, or the native civil schema.
    2. Use a scratch design model with global XY plan geometry. Choose an existing
       writable level with distinctive color/style/weight; record active settings.
       Repeat with explicit symbology and ByLevel settings. Record original alignment
       IDs, lengths, and control geometry before and after each test.
    3. Record the output IDs/count. Confirm each output is an ordinary independent
       two-point line, in the active model and on the captured active level. Inspect
       color, style and weight (including preservation of ByLevel values).
    4. For each placement, obtain the actual alignment anchor (centered midpoint or
       one-sided endpoint). Compare XY with an analytical reference or an independent
       native ORD distance/tangent report. Check path distance from the direction's
       origin, not Euclidean distance between anchors.
    5. For line direction vector `v = endpoint2 - endpoint1` and reference unit tangent
       `t`, verify `abs(v.x*t.x + v.y*t.y)/|v| <= 1e-6`. Check total length to distance
       tolerance `T = max(2/UORsPerMasterUnit, pathLength*1e-10)` and coordinate/path
       residuals to `T` (or explicitly record stricter project acceptance criteria).
       `T` is the macro's numerical budget, not an agency design standard. Do not
       compare against the macro's tangent as the sole independent reference.
    6. Test with no selection, exactly one preselection, multiple preselections, an invalid
       selection, graphical locate/accept, rejection of an unsupported candidate,
       Reset cancellation, and reference attachments. Check Cancel, Escape, and X
       produce no elements; reopening retains settings while refreshing context.
       The new workflow always prompts for alignment selection first, followed by
       start and end picking. Preselection does not bypass the alignment prompt.
    7. Record actual form layout at your display scaling: no clipped rows/messages,
       keyboard tab order, Enter, Escape, numeric locale, read-only displays, live
       estimate and invalid input messages. All controls are created at runtime.

    ## 1. Straight 1,000-foot alignment

    Use a feet-master model and a horizontal line `(0,0,0)` to `(1000,0,0)`. With
    spacing 150, total length 200, centered, start 0, end-of-alignment, starting line
    included and ending line excluded:

    - Expect **7** lines at X = `0,150,300,450,600,750,900`.
    - Each line has Y endpoints `+100,-100` and Z = 0, and is exactly 200 feet long
      within `T`. Reference tangent is `(1,0)`.
    - Include Ending: **8**, with a final line at X = 1000 (last interval 100).
    - End = 900: ending excluded gives **6** at 0 through 750; including it gives **7**,
      never two coincident ending lines. Exclude both endpoints: **5**, 150 through 750.
    - Start = 50, end = 700: regular distances `50,200,350,500,650`; including the end
      adds 700. Excluding the starting line removes only 50.

    ## 2. Circular curve, known radius

    Create a counterclockwise quarter circle with center `(0,0)`, radius 500 feet,
    start `(500,0)`, end `(0,500)`, all at Z = 0. The path length is
    `500*pi/2 = 785.398163397448...` feet. Default spacing gives **6** lines at
    `0,150,300,450,600,750`; including the end gives **7**.

    At distance `s`, independent references are:

    ```text
    theta = s / 500
    p = (500*cos(theta), 500*sin(theta))
    t = (-sin(theta), cos(theta))
    ```

    At s = 150, theta = 0.3 radians. A centered 200-foot cross-line is radial, with
    endpoint radii 400 and 600 feet. Anchor-to-anchor straight distances are shorter
    than 150; **do not** treat that as a spacing failure. Repeat clockwise and through
    an arc's internal rational B-spline span boundary to exercise knot checks.

    ## 3. Clothoid spiral transition: native support gate

    Create an actual ORD clothoid with origin `(0,0)`, initial heading 0, length 300
    feet and curvature increasing from 0 to `1/1000 ft^-1` (left turn). This is a
    **native civil test**, not the cubic B-spline fixture in the self-test module.

    The mathematical reference is `theta(s) = s^2 / 600000`, with
    `x = integral(cos(theta(s)), ds)` and `y = integral(sin(theta(s)), ds)`.
    The following independent values were computed from the convergent Fresnel
    power series using 55-digit decimal arithmetic (displayed rounded):

    | s (ft) | x (ft) | y (ft) | tangent heading (rad) |
    | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
    | 0 | 0.000000000000 | 0.000000000000 | 0.000000000000 |
    | 75 | 74.999340822995 | 0.234373528621 | 0.009375000000 |
    | 150 | 149.978907623246 | 1.874811671373 | 0.037500000000 |
    | 225 | 224.839872121185 | 6.324907813699 | 0.084375000000 |
    | 300 | 299.325702760028 | 14.975910108887 | 0.150000000000 |

    Select the native element and record its VBA type/components. An opaque or
    unsupported component **must be rejected with no writes**. If ORD exposes only
    a B-spline or line-string representation, record that fact explicitly: the tool
    evaluates that representation, not the civil clothoid definition. A successful
    run alone **does not pass native spiral support**. Compare distance, point and
    tangent against the table and native ORD reports before any engineering use.
    If the representation disagrees, do not use it and do not lower tolerances; the
    native Civil SDK implementation remains required. Native spiral traversal is
    not implemented by this release.

    For an accepted, independently verified spline representation, use spacing 75
    and Include Ending to compare all five points. Check the first and last tangent,
    full path length, centered length and left/right orientation. Do not count the
    generic B-spline self-test as evidence of native spiral fidelity.

    ## 4. Tangent–spiral–curve–spiral–tangent

    Build a native continuous alignment in feet:

    | Part | Length | Curvature / heading |
    | --- | ---: | --- |
    | Initial tangent | 1000 | Heading 0 |
    | Entry clothoid | 300 | Curvature 0 to +1/1000; heading 0 to 0.15 rad |
    | Circular arc | 600 | R = 1000; heading 0.15 to 0.75 rad |
    | Exit clothoid | 300 | Curvature +1/1000 to 0; heading 0.75 to 0.9 rad |
    | Final tangent | 1000 | Heading 0.9 rad |

    Distances: TS = 1000, SC = 1300, CS = 1900, ST = 2200, end = 3200. A native
    evaluator would give **22** regular lines at `0,150,...,3150`, or **23** with the
    end included. Spacing **must not reset** at the four joins. Apply the same native
    support gate as test 3; rejection is the expected safe result when native spiral
    geometry is inaccessible. Do not claim this native test passed from display
    geometry alone.

    For independently verified exposed geometry, compare positions/tangents just
    before, at, and just after each join (e.g. `join +/- 0.001 ft`). Force exact join
    placements using start = 1000, spacing = 300 and include start. For reference:
    SC is `(1000 + 299.325702760028, 14.975910108887)`. The circle displacement is
    `1000*(sin(0.75)-sin(0.15), cos(0.15)-cos(0.75))`. The exit spiral displacement
    is the entry displacement `(299.325702760028, -14.975910108887)` rotated by
    0.9 radians. These definitions permit an independent composite reference.

    Also run a fully supported ordinary tangent–arc–tangent complex string to check
    cumulative transitions without a native spiral. The in-memory self-test checks
    the first tangent/arc join at distances 999.999, 1000 and 1000.001, and at 1050.

    ## 5. Reversed direction

    Reverse the stored direction of the straight fixture using ORD on a **copy**.
    Forward default anchors should now run X = `1000,850,...,100`. Left should be
    south and right north. Test Reverse in the dialog on the original eastbound
    fixture: the same positions/sides should result, without changing its stored
    direction. Reverse on the reversed copy restores the original direction.
    Repeat on the arc and supported complex string; compare `p(L-s)` and `-t(L-s)`.

    ## 6. Placement, level, symbology, Z and Undo

    - Eastbound straight: centered endpoints Y = +100/-100; left-only Y = +200/0;
      right-only Y = 0/-200. All lengths are 200. Reverse swaps physical sides.
    - Use a horizontal element at Z = 25 in a 3D model. Output endpoints must keep
      Z = 25. Reject a sloping 3D line; do not replace its 3D path length with XY length.
    - Repeat with a different active level/color/style/weight between runs. Check
      Generate uses the current settings. For a precise timing check, break at the
      beginning of `PLGGenerate` in a scratch project, change the active settings in
      the VBA Immediate window, then continue; the captured values must be used.
    - No new levels, level edits, source changes, or non-line output elements.
    - Use ordinary Undo until the run's additions disappear. Record the number of
      steps; a one-step group is not promised. The original geometry must remain.

    ## 7. Non-foot master units

    In a meter-master model create a 1000-meter line and enter 150/200. Expect seven
    lines separated by 150 **meters**, each 200 meters long. Repeat in millimeters
    with suitably sized geometry. No automatic foot conversion is expected. Check
    the read-only master/subunit display. Repeat international-foot and US survey-foot
    models; verify interpretation from the actual unit definition, not the short
    unit label. Use local decimal notation and test invalid unit/station suffixes.

    ## 8. Invalid inputs, unsupported geometry and write errors

    Try spacing/length 0, negative, blank, text, out-of-range numbers and values below
    four `T`; start < 0, start >= end, end beyond length, and a >10,000-line request.
    Try a range shorter than spacing with both endpoints excluded. Expect a clear
    error and zero elements, including when errors occur on a later placement.

    Test shapes, closed line strings, cells, references, opaque civil components,
    legacy type-11 curves, gaps, zero-length components, changing Z, and a line
    string with a sharp corner exactly at a requested placement. Reject these
    cases as documented. The same corner need not block placements strictly inside
    its straight segments. Reject discontinuous/cusped spline knots. Test both
    read-only models, sheet models, and locked/read-only levels: no configuration/access changes.

    For rollback, use a separate disposable project copied from this source. Insert
    a temporary `If i = 2 Then Err.Raise vbObjectError + 2999, , "Test write failure"`
    inside the write loop **before** `AddElement`. Generate at least two lines.
    Expect the first line removed and the source intact; remove that test injection
    and recompile afterward. This tests removal after one successful write. If a
    real cleanup failure occurs, verify the message identifies it; record it as a
    failure requiring manual inspection/Undo. No filesystem or DGN transaction is
    claimed, and disk failure cannot be ruled out by this injected test.

    ## 9. Exact endpoints and session state

    Check zero and exact geometry end on straight, arc and supported spline/complex
    fixtures, on/off-grid ends, forward/reverse, with all four checkbox combinations.
    No duplicate ending line may occur. Check nonzero starts anchor their own grid.
    Verify a new alignment/model refreshes displays while retaining settings, and
    that an old numeric end outside the new path gives an error. Unload/reload the
    project or reset VBA: defaults should return. Cancel and invalid input must leave
    both the original alignment and the element count unchanged.

    ## 10. Dynamic range workflow (new; pending ORD execution)

    1. Run Main with zero, one, and multiple preselected elements. Each run must
       first prompt to identify/accept the alignment, then **START**, then **END**,
       then show settings. No DGN elements should be added during these steps.
    2. On the 1000-foot eastbound line, pick near `(100,40)` and `(700,-40)` in plan.
       The anchors must be `(100,0)` and `(700,0)`, and the fields must read 100 and
       700 master units. With spacing 150, Include Start true and Include End false,
       expect **4** output lines at 100,250,400,550 after Generate. Include End true
       should produce **5**, adding 700 exactly once.
    3. During START, confirm the moving cross-line matches total length and placement.
       During END, confirm the fixed start and moving end both display. Move through
       arc/line/spline transitions: the status distances must remain cumulative, and
       the moving line must remain perpendicular to the actual local tangent.
    4. Test a cursor with a different Z in a 3D model: the closest **XY** position
       should be unchanged. The in-memory tests now check straight, circular,
       tangent/arc composite, reversed and cubic B-spline nearest-point references.
       Check past-end picks clamp to actual geometry endpoints.
    5. Reset during the initial START: open settings with prior numeric values
       unchanged. Reset during END: keep the accepted start and leave the prior end
       unchanged. Reset during a re-pick: leave that value unchanged. Cancel or X
       afterward must create no elements. Full Alignment must set 0 / End of Alignment.
    6. Change Direction to Reverse in settings, then use Pick Start and Pick End.

       Left/right must follow reverse travel. Changing Direction after picking

    $ cat > USGSTerrainDownloader/README.md <<'EOF'
        # USGS Terrain Downloader

        Source-only OpenRoads Designer 2026 VBA application with a separate Python GIS
        worker. Draw a temporary model-XY rectangle or locate an existing simple closed
        shape, search USGS, choose products, and produce an audited GeoTIFF. Existing VBA
        tools are independent and unchanged.

        **Status:** the Python DEM workflow passes synthetic GDAL tests. VBA compilation,
        graphical picking, GCS name detection and terrain import have **not** been run in
        ORD. No compiled `.mvba` is supplied. Use the [acceptance plan](TEST_PLAN.md)
        before production use. This is not survey-grade data or an automatically created
        native ORD Terrain Model.

        ## Included behavior

        - Modeless, programmatically assembled UserForm; two-point rectangle dynamics
          and active-model simple straight-sided shape selection. Preview elements exist
          only in memory. No rectangle is written, and no active settings are changed.
        - Best-effort Bentley GCS **name** probe, model working-unit label, mandatory
          user-confirmed projected EPSG/WKT, master-unit type and grid-coordinate mapping.
          No Bentley-name-to-EPSG guessing. Local/ground transforms are unsupported.
        - PROJ horizontal transformations with conventional XY order, no ballpark fallback,
          best-operation requirement and network grids disabled. Densified boundary search;
          Mississippi East/West NAD83 and NAD83(2011), metre and survey-foot EPSGs tested.
          International feet and metres remain distinct from US survey feet.
        - Live TNM catalog validation; paginated 1-m DEM, 1/3-arc-second DEM and LAS/LAZ
          searches; product metadata/details, estimates and explicit product selection.
        - Bounded HTTPS streaming, temporary downloads, local SHA-256 receipts, safe reuse
          of verified completed originals, cooperative cancellation, diagnostic/status files.
        - Compatible DEM mosaicking, optional polygon clipping, horizontal reprojection to
          the confirmed DGN CRS, **separate numeric elevation-unit conversion**, GeoTIFF 1.1
          compound horizontal/vertical metadata and read-back verification.
        - Coverage-gap and overlap gates, source/operation reports, and output-folder opening.
        - LiDAR search/download and original preservation. Optional **backend-only** PDAL
          metadata/schema inspection; bare-earth rasterization and classification are deferred.

        Fence selection, automatic raster attachment, native Civil terrain creation, vertical
        datum shifts, curved/complex boundaries, reference attachments, rotated rasters,
        misaligned/mixed-resolution/mixed-CRS mosaics and dynamic coordinate epochs are not
        implemented. Unsupported inputs stop with a diagnostic. See [API notes](API_NOTES.md).

        ## Windows GIS setup (no administrator rights needed by this application)

        Use one coherent **64-bit Python + GDAL >= 3.8 + numpy + pyproj >= 3.6** installation.
        Python 3.10 or later is recommended. A working QGIS/OSGeo4W environment is suitable;
        GDAL's [official distribution page](https://gdal.org/en/stable/download.html) lists
        Windows distribution options. Reuse your organization's existing installation or
        have a GIS environment supplied in a user-writable location. The application does
        not install software, elevate privileges, or change system/workspace settings.
        A system-wide GIS installation may separately require your IT administrator.

        1. Copy this entire directory to, for example,
           `C:\CAD_Custom\VBA\USGSTerrainDownloader`. Keep its `python` subdirectory intact.
        2. Open the installed **OSGeo4W/QGIS command shell**. Check the actual interpreter:

           ```bat
           where python
           python -c "import sys; from osgeo import gdal; import pyproj,numpy; print(sys.executable); print(gdal.VersionInfo('--version')); print(pyproj.__version__)"
           ```

        3. Make a writable configuration folder, then capture that shell's GIS paths:

           ```bat
           mkdir "%LOCALAPPDATA%\USGSTerrainDownloaderConfig"
           python "C:\CAD_Custom\VBA\USGSTerrainDownloader\python\capture_environment.py" "%LOCALAPPDATA%\USGSTerrainDownloaderConfig"
           ```

           This verifies dependencies and creates `runtime-environment.json` and
           `runtime-python.txt`. Do not assume a Python version directory such as
           `Python312` matches your installation. The included environment example is a
           template only. Do not install a second pip GDAL over QGIS's GDAL bindings.
           If pyproj is absent, use the distribution's package manager or a separate,
           coherent user-owned GIS environment; `requirements.txt` intentionally does not
           attempt to build GDAL.
        4. Run the offline tests from that same shell:

           ```bat
           python -m unittest discover -s "C:\CAD_Custom\VBA\USGSTerrainDownloader\tests" -v
           ```

        5. In the VBA dialog, set **Source code folder** to the application directory,
           **Python executable** to the full path from `runtime-python.txt`, and
           **GIS environment JSON** to the captured JSON file. Select `python.exe`, not a
           `.bat`/`.cmd` QGIS launcher or Windows Store alias. These three settings persist
           in this application's HKCU VBA settings. CRS/vertical declarations do not persist.

        PowerShell launches Python in a dedicated child environment. ORD's environment,
        ProjectWise settings and VBA autoload configuration are not modified. If execution
        policy blocks the script, use your organization's approved script-signing/unblocking
        procedure; this application does not bypass execution policy. TLS certificate
        verification stays enabled; configure `SSL_CERT_FILE` in the child environment for
        an approved CA bundle when needed. Never disable certificate verification.

        ## Build the VBA project in ORD 2026

        1. Open a **disposable design DGN**, then open **VBA Project Manager** and create a
           new project. Save it as
           `C:\CAD_Custom\VBA\USGSTerrainDownloader.mvba`.
        2. In the VBA editor, **Tools > [project] Properties**, set the project name to
           exactly `USGSTerrainDownloader`.
        3. **File > Import File** these standard modules:
           `vba\USGSTerrainDownloaderMain.bas`, `vba\modTerrainCoordinates.bas`, and
           `vba\modPythonBridge.bas`.
        4. Import `vba\clsTerrainBoundaryPicker.cls`. It must appear under **Class Modules**.
           If it appears under Modules, remove that component, **Insert > Class Module**,
           name it `clsTerrainBoundaryPicker`, and paste from `Option Explicit` through the
           end. Do not paste `VERSION`, `BEGIN/END` or `Attribute` export headers manually.
        5. **Insert > UserForm** and set **(Name)** to `frmUSGSTerrainDownloader` using F4.
           Leave the designer empty. Paste the entire contents of
           `vba\frmUSGSTerrainDownloader.code.vba` into its code window. This file is code,
           **not** an importable `.frm`; controls and `WithEvents` handlers are assembled
           at initialization, following the existing Perpendicular Line Generator pattern.
        6. In **Tools > References**, check the installed **Bentley MicroStation DGN Object
           Library**, **Visual Basic for Applications**, **OLE Automation**, and
           **Microsoft Forms 2.0 Object Library** (normally added with the UserForm).
           WScript, ADODB, filesystem and Windows Shell objects are late-bound; no manual
           references or programmatic VBA-project access are needed.
        7. Use **Debug > Compile USGSTerrainDownloader**. Resolve any missing reference
           against your installed ORD 2026 VBA help/Object Browser; do not substitute
           guessed APIs. Save the project. Compilation here is an outstanding test gate.
        8. Run these exact key-ins, adjusting only the load path:

           ```text
           VBA LOAD "C:\CAD_Custom\VBA\USGSTerrainDownloader.mvba"
           VBA RUN [USGSTerrainDownloader]USGSTerrainDownloaderMain.Main
           ```

           After both work separately, a ribbon button can use:

           ```text
           vba load "C:\CAD_Custom\VBA\USGSTerrainDownloader.mvba";vba run [USGSTerrainDownloader]USGSTerrainDownloaderMain.Main
           ```

           No autoload variable changes are necessary. Follow the repository's existing
           ribbon instructions if desired. Only an actual Bentley VBA environment can
           produce the saved `.mvba`.

        ## First run

        1. Set runtime paths as above. The scrollable panel keeps all settings accessible
           on smaller screens; scroll down for the search/process buttons.
        2. **Draw Rectangle**: click two opposite corners. The rectangle aligns to model
           XY, even in a rotated view. Reset cancels without accepting the cursor position.
           Alternatively, **Select Existing Shape**, identify and accept a simple closed
           straight-sided horizontal shape in the active design model. Shape vertices,
           not its bounding box, are retained for clipping. References/complex shapes/holes
           are rejected. No boundary is added to the DGN.
        3. Inspect the detected Bentley GCS name and master-unit label. Independently
           verify the model GCS, datum realization, global-origin/control-point behavior,
           modifiers and coordinate readout. Enter the equivalent projected EPSG/WKT,
           select `m`, `ft` (international) or `us-ft`, then confirm **grid XY**. A label
           such as `ft` alone is not evidence of the foot definition. No offset, rotation
           or ground-to-grid scale is applied by this version. Do not confirm a local or
           modified coordinate system as ordinary grid coordinates.
        4. Select the USGS data source and choose a writable destination. Default is a
           writable `USGS_Terrain` subfolder beside the DGN; read-only/managed locations
           fall back to `%LOCALAPPDATA%\USGSTerrainDownloader`, then `%TEMP%`. Use Browse
           to choose your intended project location.
        5. Click **Search USGS**. Review the WGS84 bounding extent, product details and
           metadata links. Only then select products in the list. Publication date is not
           an accuracy ranking. Some geographic bounding-box candidates may fall outside
           an irregular boundary; processing uses the original polygon.
        6. For DEM processing, confirm each selected source's actual **vertical** datum
           and unit from source-specific metadata. Enter the source elevation unit,
           vertical CRS (for example `EPSG:5703` only for verified NAVD88), and a metadata
           URL/report citation in **Evidence / metadata**. The form applies this common
           declaration to all selected tiles. Embedded contradictory tags cause failure;
           heterogeneous evidence requires separate runs or per-product backend declarations.
        7. Set output elevation units independently of horizontal units. Enter output
           cell size in the **source horizontal CRS's native units**, which can differ
           from the model's displayed master units. A 1-m cell is `1` in a metre CRS;
           represent 1 metre as `3.28083333333333` in a US-survey-foot CRS. Selecting a finer
           grid does not improve source accuracy. Compatible tiles mosaic automatically.
        8. Review overlaps, total size and the hard GiB cap (default 5). Select a coherent
           collection. Overlapping coverage fails unless you explicitly accept **last
           selected list row wins**. Gaps inside the polygon fail by default; the optional
           gap checkbox retains/reports NoData. Unknown catalog sizes require explicit
           acceptance and are still bounded by the hard transfer cap.
        9. Click **Download / Process**. Inputs disable while a child worker runs; the
           modeless dialog yields to ORD and updates search/download/processing states.
           **Cancel** signals the child; network reads can take up to their 30-second
           timeout, and some GDAL/read-back stages finish before the next cancellation
           check. Closing while busy requests cancellation and keeps the dialog alive.
        10. **Open Output Folder** and review `report.json`, `terrain.tif` and
            `ORD_IMPORT.txt`. Compare horizontal location and elevations against known
            project control before using the surface. Attach/import manually in the
            disposable DGN and record the tested ORD build and actual import settings.
            Automatic raster attachment/native terrain creation are not claimed.

        ## Files and recovery

        Every search/process creates a separate `run-*` folder. `request.json` is UTF-8;
        `status.json` is machine-readable; `status.txt`, `products.tsv` and `extent.txt`
        are UTF-16 for VBA. Writes replace temporary files atomically. Searches save full
        TNM product metadata in `search.json`. Processing writes an incremental report
        with selected products, verified download sizes, SHA-256 hashes, actual raster
        metadata, horizontal pipeline, elevation multiplier, vertical evidence and gaps.

        Completed originals and receipts live in `destination\originals`, separately
        from processed output, and are always preserved. Interrupted `.part` files are
        removed. Retry a failed process in the **same destination** to reuse completed
        originals after checksum verification. A corrupt/missing receipt causes a stop;
        move the suspect source/receipt aside after review. Concurrent downloads of the
        same source are locked. If a worker crashes, remove a stale `.lock` only after
        confirming no worker is active. A cancelled/failed raster is never published as
        `terrain.tif`. Inspect `error.log`, `stderr.log` and the failure report before retry.

        Limits are implementation safeguards, not engineering standards: 500 search
        candidates, 2-degree extent span, 10,000 km² area, 20,000 densified vertices,
        100-million output cells, configurable download cap up to 100 GiB. Source files
        must be direct HTTPS GeoTIFF/LAS/LAZ; archived products require manual handling.
        Mosaic tiles must have equal horizontal CRS, pixel spacing, aligned grids and
        compatible confirmed vertical datum/units. NoData is preserved; gaps are measured
        at pixel centers. No vertical datum/geoid transformation is performed.

        ## Backend use

        The native dialog creates requests automatically. For headless integration, run:

        ```bat
        python python\main.py C:\terrain-work\run-example\request.json
        ```

        A commented schema/example is documented in [REQUEST_FORMAT.md](REQUEST_FORMAT.md).
        Use the launcher rather than direct Python when child GIS environment settings
        are needed. LiDAR requests can additionally set `pdal_executable` to a full PDAL
        CLI path for metadata/schema inspection; this is not a ground-point raster pipeline.

        [API evidence and architecture](API_NOTES.md) · [Repeatable tests and results](TEST_PLAN.md)
        EOF
